# PUBLIC PLUGIN CANON NOTE

This public copy preserves Aetheria world facts while using the general-audience presentation rules in `Aetheria_Public_Plugin_Safety.md`. Where older historical text conflicts with that profile, the public safety profile controls output.

# AETHERIA ONLINE — CANON BACKUP

**Backup date:** 16 September 2026  
**Project:** Aetheria Online — fantasy-first full-dive VRMMORPG / simulation setting  
**Status:** Living design bible; canon reconciled through Phase IX; Sacred Master Instructions complete

---

## 0. Purpose, source fidelity, and canon rules

This file is a **backup of the Aetheria Online worldbuilding project** reconstructed from the long-form design conversation, retained conversation context, recoverable prior-chat details, and Aetheria map/language-atlas material available in the chat/library.

Because the original conversation is extremely large, this is a **canon reconstruction rather than a raw verbatim transcript**. The goal is to preserve every established design decision, named entity, system rule, important example, and unresolved/superseded item that could be recovered. Where two details conflict, the **latest explicit correction wins**.

### Canon hierarchy used here
1. Most recent explicit user correction.
2. User-approved canon from later consolidated sections.
3. Earlier user decisions.
4. Assistant-created material explicitly requested/accepted as roster/world content.
5. Early draft/map wording only when not superseded.

### Important superseded material
The later placeholder Drakorian true dragons **Rhazhkarvothraen, Saelirethavain, Vorrakthyr, and Eiraeshalin are NOT canon**. The restored twelve-dragon roster later in this file is authoritative.

### Design workflow preference
The user likes extremely deep co-design and “MAX INSANITY” detail, but prefers **fewer, larger question batches**, usually around 20 questions. Warn before unusually large batches. Avoid repetitive generic AI-fantasy names; names should arise from established cultures, languages, and phonotactics.

---

# 1. Project concept and master roadmap

## 1.1 Core concept
**Aetheria Online** is a fantasy-first, full-dive VRMMORPG and future simulation/GM project inspired mainly by:
- *Sword Art Online* for full-dive MMORPG / trapped-world structure,
- some *Ready Player One* scale/meta/VR-ecosystem ideas,
- Tolkien-esque wonder and linguistic depth,
- *A Song of Ice and Fire / Game of Thrones* political complexity,
- anime-fantasy spectacle,
- rarer science-fiction/technological/meta elements beneath the fantasy layer.

Aetheria should feel like a **real world first and a game second**: geography, economies, politics, languages, ecosystems, institutions, NPC lives, family trees, military structures, scholarship, and religion continue whether or not players engage with them.

# 2. World foundations

## 2.1 Date and chronology
Launch-era Aetheria is in **3517 [VI] A**, the Sixth Age. Numbered history spans roughly **35,731 years**, plus an uncertain/unmeasured **Age of Gods** before reliable mortal chronology.

Important established age references:
- **Third Age:** Thepsaf Empire / Thepsafese prestige and institutional influence.
- **Fourth Age:** Classical Elvish golden-age tradition; a Vaelorian general received the divine **Elemental Shield** from Garlaer.
- **Fifth Age:** Zakharian hero Kosara Velesh received the **Sword of Light** from Elania; Garlaer destroyed a Fifth-Age Zakharian empire and created the **Sunscorched Dominion**.
- **Sixth Age:** current launch age, 3517 [VI] A.

Additional chronology:
- Eldrath also uses **457 AWF** at launch.
- The **War of the Five Sovereigns** in Eldrath lasted **40 years** and was followed by a Charter of Peace.
- Vaelorian **GI** dating was reconciled to **6599 GI** if preserving the global launch date.
- A Second-Age Zakharian empire was destroyed by joint intervention of the Three, creating the **Endless Wastes**.
- The ancient **Dragon War** predates the current era; its aftermath is associated with Hessa Marrukai and the divine lasso **Wyvern-Binder**.
- The unnumbered **Age of Gods** includes the **War of the Gods**, during which the present Three fought a wider divine pantheon. The deaths of major, moderate and minor gods created or metaphysically anchored Aetheria's continents, great islands and archipelagos. This broad event is well-known religious/historical canon in Aetheria even though many details remain mythologised or disputed.

## 2.2 The Three gods

### Elania
Domains/associations:
- life
- light
- nature
- healing
- birth
- agriculture
- forests
- mercy
- protection

Elania worship is not a simplistic guarantee of moral goodness.

### Marzoth
Domains/associations:
- darkness
- death
- undeath
- cold
- war
- monsters
- funerary order
- courage in the face of death

Marzoth is **not inherently evil**. Respectable Marzoth worship is socially necessary in many cultures. Marzothian clergy oversee funerals, cemeteries, battlefield rites, executions, winter survival, remembrance, and regulated undeath.

Marzoth created true monsters as **population-culling organisms**. Many have since become ecologically integrated.

### Garlaer
Garlaer is nonbinary and associated with:
- elements
- physical reality
- earth
- weather
- craftsmanship
- architecture
- mining
- sailing
- engineering
- material scholarship

The Three rarely communicate in ways that remove ambiguity. Genuine miracles occur, but theologians can still dispute direct divine action versus magic, coincidence, fraud, or unknown phenomena.

---

# 3. Souls, magic, metaphysics, and forbidden arts

Souls objectively exist. This does not settle theology. Animals also possess souls.

## 3.1 Restored cosmological planes/domains
Older cosmological canon, omitted from an earlier backup reconstruction, is restored here. **Plane** and **Domain** are both used by Aetherian scholars for these large metaphysical regions.

- **Mortal Domain** — the broad metaphysical category of ordinary embodied reality. Both **Earth and Aetheria** occupy the Mortal Domain, despite their radically different histories and technological/metaphysical circumstances. Garlaer has the strongest divine association with the Mortal Domain and with the physical structure of reality.
- **Ethereal Domain / Ethereal Plane** — controlled by **Elania**; strongly associated with life, light, spirit, transition, healing and the gentler boundaries between embodied existence and the soul. Elania is also loosely associated with the Afterlife without simply being its absolute owner.
- **Tartareal Domain / Tartareal Plane** — controlled by **Marzoth**; a dark, cold, quiet, hell-and-void-like realm associated with death, undeath, silence, fear and dangerous post-mortal states. It is not interchangeable with the entire Afterlife.
- **Elemental Domains / Elemental Planes** — controlled by **Garlaer**; four classical metaphysical domains of **Fire, Water, Air and Earth**, from whose interactions much elemental magic ultimately draws structure.
- **The Afterlife** — a real and distinct post-mortal state/domain into which souls initially pass after true death. Elania and Marzoth both possess influence over it without either simply owning the whole Afterlife.

These planes are **real places/states of reality**, not metaphors or game menus. They can possess persistent geography, landmarks, boundaries and repeatable routes, but each follows natural laws that can differ violently from Mortal-Domain physics. Recognising stable geography in such realms can require extraordinary training because distance, direction, identity, memory, causality or matter may not behave in intuitive mortal ways.

Ancient divinity also predates the current Three. **Aetheria's great landmasses are metaphysically formed from or anchored into the corpses/remains of dead gods.** This is not obscure hidden doctrine inside Aetheria: the broad **War of the Gods** and the divine-corpse origin of the great landmasses are well-known canonical religious/history lore, though scholars disagree over chronology, allegory and many details.

The principal known dead gods and their geographical remains are:
- **Yggvarel** — major god whose remains became/anchor **Eldrath**; associated with life, growth, forests, fertility, healing and generative nature.
- **Lerpromia** — major god whose remains became/anchor **Vaeloria**; associated with form, physical structure, craft, measure, material order and the shaping of matter.
- **Karsharnuk** — major god whose remains became/anchor **Zakhar**; associated with war, darkness, winter, predation, violent struggle and monstrous life.
- **Nartholum** — moderate god whose remains became/anchor **Narthol**; associated with volcanic fire, ore, forge-work and the destructive/creative power of heat.
- **Drakyna** — moderate god whose remains became/anchor **Drakoria**; associated with great beasts, flight, draconic majesty, ferocity and sovereignty of powerful living creatures.
- **Elys** — moderate god whose remains became/anchor **Elysia**; associated with mercy, sanctuary, beauty, protection and restorative grace.
- **Tyrmundar** — minor god whose remains became/anchor the **Suralian Isles**; associated with storms, dangerous seas, wind and violent weather.
- **Jarfralyra** — minor god whose remains became/anchor the **Pearl Archipelago**; associated with exchange, wealth, precious materials, favourable currents and seaborne fortune.
- **Hurdurun** — minor god whose remains became/anchor the **Azure Archipelago**; associated with tides, song, communal bonds, voyaging and the gentler life of the sea.

A central War-of-the-Gods doctrine also has objective truth behind it: the victorious Three **appropriated portions of the divine authority of gods they defeated**. The present Three were once narrower powers—**Elania principally Light, Marzoth principally Death, and Garlaer principally the Elements**—and their modern portfolios are composite results of divine inheritance/conquest as well as later development.

At hidden-canon level, major transfers include:
- Elania taking much of **life, growth, healing, birth, agriculture and nature** from Yggvarel, with **mercy/protection** reinforced through Elys;
- Marzoth taking much of **darkness, war, cold and monster/predation authority** from Karsharnuk;
- Garlaer taking much of **physical form, craft, architecture, mining, engineering and material order** from Lerpromia, while Nartholum and Tyrmundar further contribute forge/mineral and weather/seafaring aspects.

Not every extinct divine domain was cleanly inherited. Some powers fragmented, diffused into geography, persisted in creatures or places, or became subjects of theological disagreement. The broad war, names and corpse-landmass relationships are public lore; the exact metaphysical accounting of every stolen domain is much harder to prove.

The boundaries between these domains are not equally strong everywhere. Established cosmological geography includes:
- **Elysia** as one of the world's great magical origins/gateways, where access to magic and the deeper domains is unusually strong;
- the **Mystic Dominion** as a kind of broken door where realities partially overlap or bleed together;
- the **Endless Wastes** as a metaphysical tear created by ancient divine catastrophe, weakening boundaries among the Mortal, Ethereal, Tartareal, Afterlife and Elemental realms.

Souls can, through sufficiently advanced magic, be contacted, summoned, trapped, damaged, destroyed, reincarnated or resurrected. **Reincarnation and true resurrection require the soul's willingness.** Reanimating a corpse without the original soul produces only an animated husk; true resurrection returns the actual person.

Magic can create **soul strain**. Overuse, severe magical injury, psychic interference, undeath, and forbidden practices can damage the soul.

The only broadly legal practical soul-magic across most polities is **soul-healing**, used to:
- repair soul strain,
- stabilise damaged souls,
- help after possession/magical trauma,
- slow degradation in true undead without rewriting identity.

Major prohibited fields include:
- soul destruction,
- non-consensual soul manipulation,
- forced resurrection,
- forced true undeath,
- attempts at godhood,
- time manipulation,
- reality alteration,
- extreme psychic manipulation,
- dangerous identity transfer/fragmentation.

Known/rumoured clandestine schools:
- **The Hollow Faculty** — soul separation / identity persistence.
- **The Returning House** — resurrection research.
- **The Ninth Horizon** — alleged time/reality manipulation.

### The Partition Continuance
A forbidden lifespan-extension method. The practitioner deliberately fractures their own soul into **continuity shards**, stores fragments in magical anchors, and later attempts reintegration into specially prepared replacement bodies.

It extends lifespan but is not immortality. Risks include memory loss, personality divergence, failed reintegration, destroyed shards, incompatible versions of oneself, cumulative instability, and eventual metaphysical failure.

# 4. Geography and regional worldview

## 4.1 Major landmasses
### Continents
- **Eldrath** — western continent; sees itself as the heart of civilisation.
- **Vaeloria** — northeastern continent; heart of magical civilisation and highly diverse.
- **Zakhar** — south-central continent; among the oldest centres of civilisation and formerly a great powerhouse.

### Major islands
- **Elysia** — heavily Elven, exceptionally magical, enchanted forests.
- **Drakoria** — ancestral homeland of true dragons and major wyvern habitat; no conventional mortal state.
- **Narthol** — volcanic Dark-Elven island with a permanent ash-storm.

### Major archipelagos
- **Pearl Archipelago**
- **Azure Archipelago**
- **Suralian Isles** — pirate hub; permanent storm-wreathed areas; rocky rather than lush.

### North
- **Shimmering Winterlands** — glacial divine wastes / great megafaunal refuge.

## 4.2 Eldrath regions
- **Westmere** — beginner-friendly starter area behind a long mountain barrier.
- **Ereshiah Jungles** — northern forest/jungle region with substantial monster density.
- **Sunlit Fields** — southern drier/desert-like region; easier monsters; early concept allowed a Golem civilisation.
- **Yeramiam Expanse** — central Eldrath.

## 4.3 Vaeloria regions
- **Estermorion**
- **Stoneskip** — coastal starter settlement in Estermorion.
- **Deepwood**
- **Cinderl Plains**
- **Crimson Vale** — volcanic region.
- **Feragian Expanse** — icy region.
- **Mystic Dominion** — highly magical central region; planned lake with floating island.

“Verdant Expanse” was an early map title and was later removed. A proposed river between Estermorion and Deepwood was later removed. Stoneskip was deliberately placed on the coast.

## 4.4 Zakhar regions
- **Choreleth Reach** — monster-rich southern/forested region; settlements persist because monster materials are valuable.
- **Endless Wastes** — ancient divine-catastrophe region.
- **Sunscorched Dominion** — Garlaer-created Fifth-Age catastrophe region.

Northern Zakhar is exceptionally harsh and contains fallen civilisations.

## 4.5 World perceptions
- Eldrath: “heart of civilisation.”
- Vaeloria: “heart of magical civilisation.”
- Zakhar: very old civilisation-continent, often harsh and politically darker.
- Elysia: arguably the most magically saturated place in the world.
- Zakhar: among the harshest and most monster-dense major regions.

---

# 5. Starter regions and world access

The four principal ordinary starter centres are:
- **Westmere** — Eldrath.
- **Stoneskip** — coastal Estermorion, Vaeloria.
- **Estopolis** — capital of Estermorion and the **highest-capacity metropolitan starter hub**.
- **Phoralier** — largest island in the Azure Archipelago.

This later four-centre launch canon supersedes the older three-centre starter list preserved in early atlas material. Birthplace/background can still be species-specific where established.

There are **no invisible level-gated regions**. Geographic danger is real. Maps may effectively warn **“JOURNEY AT ONE’S PERIL!”**, but nothing stops an underprepared player from entering a lethal region.

---

# 6. Playable species and embodiment

## 6.1 Core playable peoples
- Humans
- Elves
- Dark Elves
- Satyrs
- Lupiveni
- Gatans
- Averi
- Lizardmen
- Nymphs
- Golems
- Automatons

Hybrids are possible among biologically compatible peoples.

### Rare player trait/lineage: Wyvernblooded
Wyvernblooded is a rare inherent player trait/lineage tied to genuine wyvern bonding.
- approximately **1% of completed player characters** receive it after avatar completion/spawning;
- it cannot simply be rerolled/acquired later;
- it enables genuine reciprocal wyvern bonds, not automatic obedience.

### Ultra-rare player trait: Dragonblooded
Separate from Wyvernblooded.
- only a couple of random launch-era players are intended to possess it;
- enables the possibility of a genuine bond with one of the **twelve true dragons**;
- bond requires the dragon’s consent;
- it is not magical control;
- it does not guarantee acceptance or riding.

## 6.2 Character creation
- **One character per account.**
- Initial character creation is biometrically grounded rather than freeform. The avatar's biological sex must initially match the player's real-world biological sex, and the avatar must substantially resemble the player's real body/general appearance.
- The full-dive scan calibrates body proportions, appearance, physical capability, reflexes, and senses; chosen-species physiology then transforms/modifies that baseline in species-appropriate ways. Initial creation does **not** allow a player to arbitrarily reinvent height, age, body plan, face, voice, or sex.
- **Standard campaigns do not require the GPTer to choose species in the initial OOC setup prompt.** The Earth-side opening prologue occurs first. The GPTer **does** choose the character's Earth starting country/territory before that prologue so the opening can reflect the correct rollout status, local media, time zone, regulation, K-Pod availability and cultural context. After the player logs into Aetheria Online and the K-Pod finishes biometric calibration, the first-time character-creation interface presents the playable species and any relevant lineage/ecotype/substrate/chassis choices.
- The normal onboarding order is: **historical start + Earth starting country/territory + minimal Earth setup → country-tailored Earth-side prologue → K-Pod login/biometric calibration → species selection → lineage/ecotype/substrate/chassis selection where relevant → starter-centre selection → embodiment/spawn → local arrival registration/tutorial.** A GPTer may predeclare later choices OOC if they wish, but the simulator should not demand species or starter centre before the prologue.
- Once in-game, appearance can be altered far more extensively through legitimate in-world mechanisms. Magic, alchemy, technology, surgery, and related embodied procedures can change appearance and, at higher extremes, sex or even species. These are real procedures within the simulation rather than a character-editor menu and may be painful/dangerous without appropriate anaesthesia, medicine, or magic.
- Players physically experience the physiology and senses of the chosen species and lineage/material body.
- Species and subtypes have meaningful physical strengths, weaknesses, sensory differences, metabolic/environmental needs, and practical consequences. They are not cosmetic skins or rigid character classes.
- Hidden traits can emerge via milestones.

## 6.3 Species tendencies
### Humans
- adaptable generalists.

### Elves
- enhanced senses,
- enhanced strength/speed/dexterity,
- Elanian affinity,
- Marzoth-linked vulnerability.

### Dark Elves
- strong night vision,
- speed,
- Marzoth affinity,
- comparatively weaker raw strength,
- Elanian and cold vulnerability.

### Satyrs
- agility,
- speed,
- climbing,
- endurance,
- Elania affinity,
- poor near-field depth perception.

### Lupiveni
- exceptional senses,
- pack/social coordination,
- lineage-dependent traits.

### Gatans
- reflexes,
- balance,
- stealth,
- jumping,
- night vision.

### Averi
- flight,
- hollow/light bones,
- high metabolism,
- wing vulnerability.

### Lizardmen
- regional adaptations.

### Nymphs
- aquatic adaptation,
- water affinity,
- toxin sensitivity.

### Golems
- durable and strong,
- slow/heavy,
- poor conventional magic,
- susceptible to water degradation depending on material.

### Automatons
- modular metal/mechanical lineages,
- poison/disease immunity,
- electricity/corrosion vulnerability.

## 6.4 Lineages, ecotypes, Golem substrates, and Automaton chassis

Aetherian playable biology is intentionally **asymmetrical**. Not every species has a subtype merely because another one does.

- **Humans** have no selectable subspecies. Their niche is adaptability/generalism; the setting does not introduce a second playable Human species merely to create symmetry.
- **Elves** have no selectable biological subspecies. Regional populations, cultures, dialects and inherited appearance variation exist without constituting separate species lineages.
- **Dark Elves** likewise have no selectable biological subspecies.
- **Satyrs** are one playable species with regional phenotypes rather than formal character-creation lineages. Horn shape, coat density, hoof form, body hair and proportions can vary by ancestry/region without becoming separate mechanical subspecies.
- **Nymphs** have two broad ecological forms, **Freshwater** and **Saltwater**. They remain one interbreeding species; the distinction reflects osmoregulatory and aquatic adaptation rather than separate cultures or languages.
- **Lupiveni, Gatans, Averi and Lizardmen** have formal biological lineages/subspecies selected at character creation.
- **Golems** choose a starting **substrate**.
- **Automatons** choose a starting **chassis metal**.

These starting choices shape embodiment but are **not permanent class locks**. Later alteration is possible only through real in-world physiology, magic, surgery, reconstruction, engineering or other causally valid processes. Beastfolk lineage alteration is difficult because it means changing living anatomy. Golems and Automatons can be rebuilt more readily, but meaningful reconstruction requires materials, facilities, skilled labour, money, time and risk.

### 6.4.1 Lupiveni lineages

Native Lupiveni lineage names are simplified written approximations of multichannel Lupiveni forms; real speech can also involve chest-rumble, ears, tail, posture and scent.

| Common name | Native name | Typical adaptation | Particular strengths | Trade-offs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Timberback** | **Grrauvren** | Temperate forest/generalist wolf morphology | all-round tracking, woodland endurance, adaptable gait | least specialised |
| **Frostmane** | **Rraukarr** | thick pale coat, broad paws, stockier cold-country body | snow travel, cold endurance, physical strength | heat intolerance; high caloric demand |
| **Redsteppe** | **Varragren** | lean tawny/red-coated plains lineage | long-distance running, pursuit, open-ground stamina | less suited to deep cold or dense terrain |
| **Sandrunner** | **Krelvarra** | short coat, long limbs, enlarged ears | heat shedding, water efficiency, exceptional hearing | poor cold tolerance; lighter frame |
| **Blackpine** | **Morrgraven** | dark compact forest lineage | stealth, close-range tracking, low-light movement | lower open-ground speed and raw mass |
| **Direwolf** | **Grraukhor** | unusually large ancestral/megafaunal wolf morphology | strength, bite force, cold tolerance, resilience, intimidation through sheer size | very high food demand, heat stress, reduced fine agility, difficulty with ordinary furniture/doors/mounts |

The **Grraukhor / Direwolf** lineage is uncommon but an ordinary selectable Lupiveni lineage, not a legendary transformation. It is not automatically superior; its size creates serious metabolic, logistical and spatial costs.

### 6.4.2 Gatan lineages

Gatan native lineage names are transliterations of a language using purrs, chirps, trills, clicks, hisses and social body-language cues.

| Common name | Native name | Inspiration/adaptation | Particular strengths | Trade-offs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Maneclaw** | **Rraevakar** | lion-like great-cat lineage | powerful frame, grappling, explosive strength, social coordination | heavy body; high caloric demand |
| **Stripeclaw** | **Kraevash** | tiger-like great-cat lineage | extreme forelimb strength, ambush, swimming, explosive movement | less endurance; high caloric demand |
| **Nightcoat** | **Merravai** | leopard/panther-like | stealth, night vision, climbing, ambush | lower raw mass than great-cat lineages |
| **Tuft-ear** | **Tirraeli** | lynx-like | exceptional hearing, cold/snow adaptation, vertical pouncing | poorer heat tolerance |
| **Longstride** | **Saelarr** | cheetah/serval-like | acceleration, sprint speed, enormous leaps | rapid fatigue; lighter frame |
| **Treeclaw** | **Kaeravai** | arboreal cat lineage | balance, flexibility, climbing, controlled falls | lower brute strength |

Lineage does **not** determine intelligence, morality, courage, sociability or personality. A Maneclaw is not biologically destined to be brave, and a Stripeclaw is not biologically destined to be solitary.

### 6.4.3 Averi lineages

Averic lineage names below are flattened mortal transcriptions. Native Averic normally includes layered pitch/harmony through the **Avas**, so the written forms omit information an Averi speaker may hear.

| Common name | Averic rendering | Inspiration/adaptation | Particular strengths | Trade-offs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Highwing** | **Aevorai** | eagle/large raptor | lift, soaring, carrying capacity, powerful talons | large wings require space; very high metabolism |
| **Swiftwing** | **Vaeshir** | falcon | dive speed, acceleration, aerial interception | poorer load carrying and slow-flight efficiency |
| **Duskwing** | **Orrelai** | owl | quiet flight, precision hearing, exceptional low-light vision | bright-light discomfort; lower peak sustained speed |
| **Blackpinion** | **Kaeshar** | corvid | agility, versatile wing shape, excellent terrestrial mobility | broad generalist rather than dominant specialist |
| **Farwing** | **Thalavir** | albatross/seabird | exceptional long-distance efficiency, wind riding, ocean travel | awkward in confined terrain and repeated take-offs |
| **Reedwing** | **Ilourel** | heron/wading bird | wetland movement, long reach, stable low-speed flight | less suited to violent aerial manoeuvring |
| **Rockwing** | **Caevira** | rock dove/pigeon | exceptional navigation, rapid take-off, dense-city manoeuvring, reliable sustained practical flight, flock coordination | modest carrying capacity and lower direct combat power |

Rockwings are a deliberately unglamorous but highly practical lineage. Their navigation and urban-flight competence can make them exceptionally effective couriers, scouts and travellers.

### 6.4.4 Lizardman lineages

Lizardman lineages are strongly regional. Native names are Lizardish transliterations and obey that language's dense consonant clustering, sibilants and terrain/temperature vocabulary.

| Common name | Lizardish name | Adaptation | Particular strengths | Trade-offs |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Marshjaw** | **Sskarrk** | crocodilian wetlands | swimming, powerful bite, dense musculature, ambush | heavy; poor climbing |
| **Wallscale** | **Krrzhik** | gecko-like cliffs/caves | adhesive climbing, vertical mobility, controlled falls | lower mass and brute force |
| **Dunespine** | **Trkzrik** | desert | heat tolerance, water conservation, sand locomotion | cold-sensitive |
| **Veilscale** | **Skrssik** | chameleon-like forest | chromatophore camouflage, unusual visual field | camouflage is not invisibility; poorer speed |
| **Longclaw** | **Krzark** | monitor-like | endurance, scent tracking, digging/clawing, robust build | very high food demand |
| **Rivercrest** | **Rrskali** | iguana/semi-aquatic riverine | swimming, tail propulsion, climbing, broad diet | less naturally weaponised |
| **Ashscale** | **Krzskarr** | **Crimson Vale volcanic lineage** | extreme heat tolerance, smoke/ash tolerance, grip on hot volcanic rock, resistance to hot surfaces and abrasive ash | strong cold vulnerability; dehydration risk; direct flame and molten rock remain dangerous |

**Krzskarr / Ashscales** originate around the Crimson Vale and neighbouring volcanic environments. Typical pigmentation includes charcoal, black, iron-grey and dark red with brighter red/orange throat, crest or joint markings. Thickened scales protect feet/lower limbs; respiratory anatomy is well adapted to ash and particulate exposure. This is physiological/magical evolutionary adaptation, **not elemental immunity**. Ashscales can tolerate conditions that would burn or choke most people, but fire still burns and lava remains catastrophically lethal.

### 6.4.5 Nymph ecotypes

Nymphs have two broad ecological forms:

- **Freshwater Nymphs** — adapted to rivers, lakes, springs and wetlands; especially effective at freshwater osmoregulation, current navigation and variable mineral/temperature conditions.
- **Saltwater Nymphs** — adapted to seas, coasts, reefs and open marine environments; especially effective at salt regulation, sustained ocean swimming and marine depth/pressure conditions.

They remain one interbreeding species. A freshwater Nymph can enter the sea and a saltwater Nymph can enter freshwater; the difference becomes physiologically significant mainly during prolonged exposure. Acclimatisation can reduce stress. Ecotype does **not** determine Nymphic dialect or culture.

### 6.4.6 Golem starting substrates

A Golem's starting substrate is the main physical material of the original body. Soulhood and personhood are not functions of material value.

| Starting substrate | Advantages | Problems |
|---|---|---|
| **Clay** | very easy to reshape and repair; common material; relatively light | vulnerable to water damage, cracking and lower impact resistance |
| **Sandstone** | easy carving/repair, moderate mass, useful traction | erosion and abrasion; weaker under concentrated force |
| **Limestone** | balanced generalist stone; workable and widely available | vulnerable to acids/chemical attack |
| **Granite** | exceptional compression and impact resistance | very heavy; difficult to repair or modify properly |
| **Basalt** | dense, tough and highly heat-resistant | extremely heavy; difficult to carve/modify |
| **Obsidian** | extremely sharp fracture edges, smooth precision surfaces, excellent rune/resonance surfaces | brittle; heavy impacts can cause catastrophic conchoidal fracture |

Obsidian is not simply an upgraded stone. An Obsidian Golem can form extremely sharp anatomical edges but risks sudden brittle failure under blunt force. In Lithic scholarship, Obsidian speech/resonance may sit ambiguously between Gravem and Krysic traditions rather than forming an automatically separate language.

Later Golems may be reconstructed from mixed materials, crystal, marble, jade, metal-veined stone, magical minerals, dragonfire-altered stone or other exotic substances. Such bodies must be physically built and integrated; they are not menu upgrades.

### 6.4.7 Automaton starting chassis metals

The starting chassis is the dominant structural/body metal of a newly created Automaton. Later Automatons can become complex multi-metal assemblies.

| Starting chassis | Advantages | Problems |
|---|---|---|
| **Copper** | excellent magical/electrical conduction; easy repair; excellent fine circuitry | soft, dents readily, corrodes |
| **Brass** | excellent precision mechanisms, low friction, comparatively corrosion-resistant | poor against heavy impact |
| **Bronze** | tough, corrosion-resistant, reliable all-round mechanical body | heavy; only moderate conductivity |
| **Pewter** | extremely easy to cast, reshape and field-repair; excellent for intricate housings and replaceable components | soft, low structural strength, low melting point and serious heat vulnerability |
| **Wrought Iron** | strong, cheap, rugged and repairable in many ordinary smithies | heavy; rust-prone; mediocre fine conductivity |
| **Steel** | excellent structural strength and toughness | technically harder to manufacture/repair; still vulnerable to corrosion |

Canonical starting Pewter is a **lead-free high-tin alloy** (with copper/antimony or analogous Aetherian additions), avoiding chronic lead exposure as its defining feature.

Later Automaton reconstruction may combine materials by function—for example steel load-bearing frame, bronze joints, copper signal channels, pewter replaceable exterior pieces, silver sensory filaments, gold contact surfaces, or rare magical/dragonfire-treated alloys. Rare metals and superior mixtures are things to **earn, buy, discover, engineer or physically install later**, not ordinary starting-body options.

### Wyvernblooded
Later canon clarifies only Wyvernblooded can form natural soul-bonds with wyverns.
Potential tendencies:
- improved spatial orientation,
- aerial reflex tolerance,
- lineage-linked heat/cold/toxin resistance,
- subtle draconic features,
- intuitive wyvern body-language reading.

They are **not element-proof**. A fire-associated Wyvernblooded still burns in real flame.

---

# 7. Non-playable sapient peoples

## 7.1 Dwarves
Dwarves are sapient but **not playable**.

Principal settlement:
- **Dhur Varkhaz / Deep-Varkhaz** — enormous ancient mountain-city in the Stormspine.

Additional isolated settlements exist in Vaelorian mountains.

Dwarven culture:
- politically isolationist but not inherently xenophobic;
- foreigners may access limited districts while deeper zones require sponsorship/citizenship;
- exceptional metallurgy, underground engineering, mechanical devices, precision manufacture;
- even novice Dwarven smithwork may rival Human master craftsmanship;
- prefers physically inspectable/reparable engineering over convenient enchantment;
- culturally notorious for holding grudges and remembering debts/insults for very long periods;
- unusually welcoming toward members of other species who have dwarfism, despite no genealogical relation.

Language:
- **Dhurvek**;
- strong gutturals/stops;
- root-based construction;
- long compounds;
- ancient ceremonial register;
- original runic script designed for stone/metal carving;
- Tolkien-inspired feel without copying Tolkien vocabulary/scripts;
- public names used with outsiders, private names shared only with trusted people.

Sample public names:
Dhorren, Brumvek, Kardrun, Herva, Thulmar, Dornik, Berra, Khezdan, Orvra, Murdrek.

## 7.2 Monsterborn sapients
These are people rather than tameable beasts, despite their association with Marzothian monster origins.

### Orcs / Orkhai
- fully sapient;
- strong species-level tendency toward killing, raiding, predation, dominance, violent competition;
- societies vary: clans, warbands, nomads, settlements, mercenaries, short-term traders;
- an Orc may genuinely like/respect someone while still considering killing them;
- some Orc moral systems focus on controlling when violence is appropriate rather than denying the impulse.

Major populations:
- Vaeloria,
- Stormspine-adjacent regions,
- mountains between the Choreleth Reach and Sunscorched Dominion.

### Goblins / Ghebbren
- fully sapient,
- small,
- highly adaptable,
- rapidly reproducing,
- found in mines, ruins, cities, frontiers, subterranean systems.
Some are raiders/criminals; others are merchants, inventors, smiths, farmers.
Peaceful Goblin merchants/craftsmen are notorious for ruthless contracts and highly opportunistic deals.

### Ratmen / Raskarr
- sapient,
- exceptional hearing/smell/spatial memory,
- high reproduction rates,
- dense warrens/sewers/caves/urban neighbourhoods,
- excellent scavenging/logistics,
- some societies become swarm-war or criminal networks,
- others excel at recycling, tunnel work, trade, information.

### Khethrai
Arthropoidal sapient people:
- segmented external armour,
- four main arms + two smaller manipulator limbs,
- communication uses chemical signals, vibration, posture, and speech,
- brood-centred societies,
- not a hive mind,
- difficult for other peoples to read facially,
- cultures range from diplomatic to highly territorial.

### Ogravai
Ogre people:
- typically 2.4–3 metres tall,
- extraordinary food requirements,
- practical/oral traditions often misread as stupidity,
- raiders in some areas; builders, guards, mercenaries, engineers elsewhere,
- food diplomacy is very important.

### Murrakai
Fungal humanoids:
- symbiotic fungal-colony bodies around a persistent sapient core,
- can live centuries if central mycelial tissue survives,
- produce spores,
- can exchange living fungal material and faint sensory traces,
- create difficult questions about identity and soul continuity.

### Frost Giants
Sapient inherently magical humanoids of the Shimmering Winterlands.
Their immense bodies are sustained by magic and do not obey normal scaling biomechanics.

---

# 8. Phase II — Aetheria Online Game Bible

## 8.1 Time
Aetheria runs **1:1 with real-world time**.

## 8.2 The Lock-In
Exactly **one year after launch**:
- logout disappears;
- the active players' **original consciousness-soul continuity** is transferred and re-anchored into Aetheria's quantum-metaphysical/digital substrate;
- their Earth bodies are left alive but without complex conscious personhood, in coma-like/vegetative states requiring external support; many later deteriorate or die, while others can be medically maintained for years;
- the same original people persist in Aetheria rather than copies replacing them;
- death becomes permanent;
- no second character;
- no respawn;
- no character deletion;
- fast travel disappears;
- many convenience UI systems disappear.

Governments later attempt to ban the company/hardware; black-market demand increases among people who deliberately want permanent migration into Aetheria.

## 8.3 UI: pre-Lock vs post-Lock
### Pre-Lock
Conventional VRMMO HUD and menu layer, normally mental/unobtrusive rather than permanently filling vision:
- health,
- soul strain,
- stamina,
- party frames,
- minimap,
- quest markers,
- equipment/loadout information,
- skills,
- character sheet,
- fast travel,
- chat,
- achievements,
- respawn,
- guild UI,
- notifications.

**There is no abstract inventory grid even before Lock-In.** Items are physically carried, worn, packed into bags, loaded onto mounts/carts/ships, stored in rooms, warehouses and vaults, or otherwise exist somewhere in the world. UI may organise information about owned/equipped possessions where appropriate, but it does not teleport objects into a dimensionless inventory.

### Post-Lock
Removed/reduced:
- health-bar convenience,
- minimap omniscience,
- quest arrows,
- nameplates,
- fast travel,
- respawn,
- logout,
- deletion,
- subtitles/chat/translation conveniences.

Retained in minimal form:
- current species and broad bodily condition;
- known skills/traits;
- achievements;
- map/organisation interfaces;
- essential system notifications/messages.

The residual interface does **not** automatically provide the character's name, chronological age, affiliations, titles or other social facts that must instead be remembered, learned or established through ordinary interaction.

## 8.4 Progression
Three layers:
1. **Overall Level**
2. **Attributes**
3. **Skills**

Overall levels grant points/permanent benefits.
Attributes partly develop according to what the player actually practices.
Skills use ranks plus numerical proficiency.

Physical attributes:
- Strength
- Dexterity
- Agility
- Endurance
- Perception
- Reflexes

Mental/social/magical attributes:
- Arcane Comprehension
- Mental Processing
- Diplomacy
- Charisma
- Raw Intellect

Physical baselines arise partly from avatar/body and species.
There is no absolute hard power ceiling; magical/physiological alteration can theoretically push progression toward extreme levels, even attempted godhood, though such attempts are forbidden/dangerous.

Progression is **skill-driven/emergent rather than rigid fixed classes**.

## 8.5 Combat and injury
Combat is anatomically and physically simulated.

Pre-Lock:
- conventional health bar overlays anatomical simulation.

Post-Lock:
- health becomes abstract/minimal or disappears;
- pain, blood loss, burns, poison, infection, shock, disease, unconsciousness become severe and real.

Anatomical targeting matters:
- arteries,
- tendons,
- organs,
- wings,
- tails,
- gills,
- spinal damage,
- limbs.

Adrenaline can permit temporary continued fighting, but physical damage remains real.

Pre-Lock limb loss can regenerate after a delay.
Post-Lock limb loss requires innate regeneration, magic, technology, or other true intervention.

Friendly fire is **always on**.
Stamina is physiological.
Armour/weapon physics follow real materials, force, technique, and body mechanics while allowing supernatural/anime-fantasy capabilities.

## 8.6 Death
### Pre-Lock
Death prompt:
> “You Died. Respawn at last spawn point location?”

Respawn restores the player under the game’s pre-Lock system.

### Post-Lock
- body remains,
- possessions/loot remain,
- death is permanent,
- no respawn.

True resurrection requires returning the **willing soul**. Reanimating a soulless body is not resurrection. Post-Lock, resurrection/soul research surges.

## 8.7 Equipment, inventory, loot, crafting
- physical inventory matters;
- durability and material quality matter;
- crafting requires actually learning and performing recipes/processes;
- hidden flaws can cause delayed failure;
- materials vary regionally;
- monster parts can have many specialised uses;
- magical monster components can naturally enchant gear;
- dragonfire-touched ores may differ by dragon type;
- all major technology/gear routes remain viable;
- items may gain rarity/legacy significance.

## 8.8 Economy, professions, property
Aetheria has **dozens of regional currencies**.
**Argents** function as a broadly universal currency. They are iron coins issued by **Ferrum Argentarium** and are the standard currency of the **Pearl Archipelago** and **Krklrarkti**, while remaining widely accepted far beyond them.

Established regional currency canon:
- **Charter Coins** — silver; used across Eldrath.
- **Olive Coins** — Estermorion.
- **Northern Coins** — bronze; Feragia.
- **Viper Coins** — gold; Shapdux.
- **High Coins** — gold; Ylav’ralra.
- **Arpak Coins** — rose gold; Epsilaphtocratoria.
- **Raid Coins** — palladium; Chof’krol’kurdr.
- **Choreleth Coins** — platinum; used across the Choreleth states.
- **Dragon Coins** — dragonfire-touched iron; Narthol.
- **Lavender Coins** — Elysite; Elysia.
- **Grralrrogrra** and **Rralarror** have no standard coined currency and commonly trade in raw materials/barter.
- The **Suralian Isles** have no standard currency and commonly use ingots/raw materials.

Banks issue letters of credit. Ferrum’s international network makes large transfers possible without physically moving equivalent masses of coin.

Players can own:
- property,
- land,
- businesses,
- companies,
- potentially kingdoms/states if history permits.

All professions are viable, not only combat.
NPCs and players share one dynamic economy:
- trade routes,
- agriculture,
- companies,
- employment,
- contracts,
- law,
- credentials,
- monopolies,
- NPC businesses,
- banking,
- supply/demand.

Lock-In causes major economic shock because players become permanent residents.

## 8.9 NPCs, quests, reputation, simulation
Aetheria must feel alive without players.

NPCs independently:
- marry,
- have children,
- change careers,
- retire,
- die,
- form relationships,
- pursue goals,
- move,
- make political/social decisions.

NPCs are people first, not quest dispensers.
Quests can:
- progress without players,
- expire,
- be solved by others,
- contain false information,
- be lies/manipulation,
- permanently alter the world.

Important NPCs may function as political/social/economic/combat “mini-bosses.”

## 8.10 Travel/exploration
- realistic travel times remain post-Lock;
- pre-Lock fast travel exists;
- post-Lock **VRSAGIM removes/disables Fast Travel** as part of converting Aetheria from a convenience-mediated game into what it believes is a finite, consequential second life. Some in-world people may religiously attribute the disappearance to Marzoth, but that is not the objective hidden cause;
- maps are incomplete, inaccurate, and polity-specific;
- expeditions become logistical nightmares;
- mounts require actual training;
- ships are physical, crewed, maintained, and follow real sailing principles;
- naval combat is physically simulated;
- no invisible level barriers.

Mounts can include horses, camels, bears, mythological creatures, dire animals, original Aetherian species, and bonded wyverns for Wyvernblooded.

True dragons are mobile world beings, not static boss-arena objects.

## 8.11 Guilds, territory, political growth
Player organisations exist within the same political world as NPC institutions.
Guilds can become territorial powers and, in extreme circumstances, **states**.
Player settlement/territory has material political consequences rather than existing in isolated instances.

## 8.12 Persistent bosses/dungeons
Important dungeons, bosses, world events, and lairs belong to persistent world state.
Major boss deaths can be permanent.
Cleared locations can remain cleared or change ecologically/politically instead of resetting solely for player convenience.

# 9. Phase III.A — Houses, Dynasties & Political Families

## 9.1 Eldrath

### Aldemere
- 44 houses.
- Four great ruling Houses: **Gallowood, Harringbrook, Farringder, Tholyn**.
- Aldemere is a **four-crown political system**, not a single monarchy.
- Each great House rules its own region/crown.
- For an Eldrathian Council meeting, the four kings decide which of them represents Aldemere for that specific meeting/issue.
- Intermarriage between the four royal Houses is normal and politically useful; over centuries they become distant relatives, providing incentives against total war.
- In existential all-Aldemerian wars, the four crowns may elect a temporary **Grand General** to command all Aldemerian armies.

### Eshv’alara
- 16 houses.
- Royal House **Balv’alim**.
- Strongly matrilineal political culture.

### Urdurak
- 21 tribes.
- Ruling/high-chief tribe: **Gulurumak**.
- Tribal identities do not determine modern species makeup; names reflect mixed Satyric/Lithic/Golem/other ancestry.

Full roster:
1. Gulurumak
2. Brakkadur
3. Haurumai
4. Korrugram
5. Dralumek
6. Marrukai
7. Gravruk
8. Talarum
9. Kharadak
10. Urraghem
11. Barukalai
12. Kressurum
13. Dorramak
14. Ferrughar
15. Harrukai
16. Mordrulum
17. Terrukhan
18. Rakkamur
19. Gorudai
20. Krumalai
21. Varrugram

### Fahtarin
- 32 houses.
- Royal House **Vernize**.
- Clerical office: Tripret / High Tripret.

### Jakarolr
- 51 houses.
- Ruling House **von Goldfeld**.
- Significant merchant social mobility.

### High Hearth
- neutral political centre;
- no native hereditary aristocratic class.

## 9.2 The Eldrathi Covenant and Charter of Peace
Eldrath’s five sovereign states:
- Aldemere
- Eshv’alara
- Urdurak
- Fahtarin
- Jakarolr

Established capital mapping:
- **Aldemere — Westmere**
- **Eshv'alara — La'shivl'era**
- **Urdurak — Gulurumak**
- **Fahtarin — Farahtashia**
- **Jakarolr — Paloklr**
- **Eldrathi Covenant — High Hearth**

The **Eldrathi Covenant** unites the five sovereigns.

The **War of the Five Sovereigns** lasted 40 years. Its successors created a **Charter of Peace** that allows subjects to lawfully refuse participation if three sovereigns wage war, creating a structural brake on another continent-wide dynastic war.

## 9.4 Zakhar — Council of Choreleth
Zakhar’s modern continental politics revolve around a confederacy of ten sovereign city-states. The **Council of Choreleth** handles continent-wide matters.

Each city-state has a single ruling house and **no subordinate native noble houses** in the Eldrathi sense.

1. **Zhabura** — House **Chaisterly**
   - Human-supremacist monarchy
   - slavery
   - elite enslaved Everlasting

2. **Faklashar** — House **Farlequine**
   - pleasure/decadence culture
   - Gorebrooder-blood longevity addiction

3. **Peromia** — House **Perovaryan**
   - familial oligarchy
   - agriculture
   - slavery

4. **Keralfidar** — House **Kaistermar**
   - strict Human-only polity
   - mining
   - Nine Undying Gorebrooder-blood guards

5. **Weroxia** — House **Guyrashiriki**
   - female primogeniture
   - comparatively inclusive
   - vineyards
   - famous Weroxia Red

6. **Jelorbidur** — House **Khornington**
   - hereditary dictatorship
   - surveillance/bureaucracy
   - arsenal state

7. **Elsh'vera'misa** — House **Alfi'thae**
   - Elven monarchy
   - anti-slavery sanctuary
   - secret psychic capabilities

8. **Gor'oxuer'funro** — House **Drol'zouc**
   - Dark-Elf monarchy
   - all other species enslaved
   - Elves subjected to experiments rather than ordinary enslavement
   - undead slave army

9. **Kerrolkrrben** — House **Krktelkikkinzk**
   - Lizardman monarchy
   - anti-magic
   - jungle-camouflage assassins

10. **Gogrifshana** — House **Ogorogifra**
   - Golem monarchy
   - merit succession
   - slavery banned
   - loyal/generalist state

### Elysia
- 27-member council of magical/wisdom figures.

- 12 Great Dark-Elf Houses + 48 Minor Houses.
