# 10. Knighting and major oaths

## 10.1 Threefold Accolade
In kingdoms worshipping the Three, a common knightly accolade is:

1. Left shoulder: **“In the name of Marzoth, I charge you to be brave.”**
2. Right shoulder: **“In the name of Garlaer, I charge you to be just.”**
3. Head: **“In the name of Elania, I charge you to defend the innocent.”**
4. Sheathe sword: **“Arise, [Name], a knight of [Kingdom].”**

## 10.2 Winterguard Oath
Canonical oath:

> “Winter gathers, and now my guard begins. It shall not end until my death. I shall take no spouse, hold no lands, and sire no children. I shall wear no crowns and win no glory. I shall live and die at my post. I am the sword in the blizzard. I am the eyes of Feragia. I am the Blazing Fire against the Shimmering Winter. I am the shield the guards the realms of Vaeloria. I pledge my life and honour to the Winterguard, for this winter, and all the winters to come.”

---

# 11. Phase III.B — Languages, Naming & Linguistics

## 11.1 Language-family foundations
- **Proto-Human** → Human languages.
- **First Speech / Proto-Elven** → Elven + Dark Elven.
- **Proto-Therian** → Lupiveni + Gatan.
- **Proto-Nymphic** independent.
- **Proto-Lizardish** independent.
- **Proto-Averic** independent.
- **Proto-Satyric** independent.
- Construct communication is separate/complicated.
- **Draconic** independent, with Srepentaigh/Draigh traditions; physiologically convergent with Lizardish in places but unrelated.
- The Three have no exclusive divine language.
- When one of the Three **directly communicates** with a mortal, the intended semantic content can be perceived across ordinary language barriers. This is a divine semantic phenomenon, not universal translation: it does not let mortals understand one another, and it can preserve metaphor, ambiguity, emotional force, deliberate obscurity, or concepts for which the listener has no clean vocabulary.
- Writing systems vary dramatically.

## 11.2 Common
- Real fictional Human language.
- Old-English-inspired with Thepsafese/Latin-ish influence.
- Aldemere and Estermorion national language; spoken in Phoralier.
- Players hear Common auto-localised into their chosen real language pre-Lock; unknown languages remain unknown.
- recognisable Common around **c. 2300 V A**.
- roughly 4,300 years old at launch.
- SVO tendency.
- 23-letter Common alphabet.

Regional/Common-register canon:
- **Aldemerian Common** is the prestige/traditional form, preserving more archaic vocabulary and formal grammar, including **thou/thee/thy** in appropriate registers. Its accent family is broadly UK-inspired. The Common-language atlas records **Westmere, Northmere, Eastmere, Central, and Southmere** varieties; Westmere specifically has an Irish-influenced melodic/lilting accent.
- **Estermorian Common** is more innovative and simplified, having largely abandoned formal **thou** in ordinary speech. Its accent family is broadly US/Canadian-inspired, with Atlantic, Central, Southern, Western and Northern regional variation.
- **Phoralian Common** is informal, relaxed, maritime and heavily influenced by Nymphic/seafaring contact. Formal **thou** is rare; Nymphic loanwords are especially common for maritime life, flora and fauna. Its accent family is broadly Australian/New-Zealand-inspired.

These forms remain mutually intelligible Common rather than separate languages. Written Standard Common is principally Aldemerian-derived, though regional spellings and usages exist.

## 11.3 Thepsafese
Dead learned language of the Third-Age Thepsaf Empire.

Features:
- 5 vowels, each short/long;
- Greek/Latin stately sound with gutturals;
- Psi /ps/;
- 8 gender classes;
- singular/dual/trial/quadral/plural;
- 8 productive cases + fossil cases;
- 6 declensions;
- extremely complex article/verb morphology;
- formal SOV;
- seven notorious irregular “Great Bastards.”

Canonical nightmare legal word:
**Ergōdōrotraumadīkomisthapodopsaronomērion**

Mystery insult:
**“three-legged fig measurer.”**

A clandestine **New Thepsaf** restoration network exists.

## 11.4 Proto-Human
- reconstructed, no confirmed writing.
- divergence around mid-Second Age.
- 5 vowels.
- modest consonant system.
- animate/inanimate seed distinction.
- 5 cases.
- singular/dual/plural.
- SOV preference.

## 11.5 Human languages

### Fahtari
- French-inspired Thepsafic descendant.
- throatier than real-world French.
- masculine/feminine.
- old number/case system mostly lost.
- silent finals, liaison, nasalisation.
- formal conservative culture.
- source of recurring “French player suffering” joke.

### Jakarolri
- 4 cases.
- 3 genders.
- V2 syntax.
- very large transparent compounds.

### Shapduxian
- Spanish + Ancient-Egyptian-inspired hybrid aesthetic.
- pharyngeals/uvulars/emphatics.
- root-pattern morphology.
- difficult orthography.
- formal/informal registers.

### Feragin
- Common is official in Feragia, but many speak archaic Feragin.
- closest major living Human language to Proto-Human.
- hard/martial sound.
- no grammatical gender.
- relatively simple grammar but difficult pronunciation.
- huge kinship/ancestry vocabulary.
- sacred Soul-tree vocabulary.

### Chorelethish
- post-Thepsafic + Ancient Zakharian + Beastfolk influence.
- reputation for ugliness.
- mostly no gender.
- structural Lupiveni influence.
- lexical Gatan influence.

### Human Azurian
- Human branch heavily reshaped by Nymphic.
- flowing.
- no gender.
- directional/spatial grammar.
- enormous maritime lexicon.

### High Forglorthian
- prestigious archaic elegant Human language.
- spreading rapidly.

### Trelk
- retains productive Proto-Western dual.

### Vask
- conservative consonants.

### Odrun
- Common/Jakarolri border code-switching.

### Gorklorki
- dying Satyr-influenced Human language.
- only hundreds of native speakers.

### Aeliri
- Human population largely shifted to Elven.

### Khedu
- isolate / controversial early Human classification.

### Tamm
- alien/substrate grammar with Proto-Human-ish core.

---

## 11.6 Elvish / First Speech
Only primary vowels:
- a
- e
- i
(no o/u in native core)

Features:
- short/long vowels + diphthongs.
- melodic, precise.
- 6 cases.
- no gender.
- singular/dual/plural.
- evidential and perspective distinctions.
- Modern Elvish remains surprisingly close to First Speech because of Elven longevity.
- apostrophes are meaningful.
- 19-letter alphabet.
- Classical Elvish is associated with a Fourth-Age golden age.

## 11.7 Dark Elvish
After the Elven schism, rapid divergence.

Features:
- vowels overwhelmingly o/u.
- dense harsh consonant clusters.
- apostrophes meaningful.
- 6 shifted cases.
- culturally prominent dual.
- no gender.
- fourth BOUND/SEALED state.
- strong forensic evidentiality.
- neutral SOV.

Major dialects:
- Nartholian
- Chof'krol'kurdr
- Gor'oxuer'funro
- diaspora varieties

21-letter alphabet.

## 11.8 Nymphic
Independent family.

Nymph anatomy:
- **Nymsac** water/air sac allows underwater phonation.

Features:
- 7–8 vowels.
- aquatic phonemes.
- pitch carries information.
- 3D spatial worldview.
- directional particles.
- serial verbs.
- movement-state noun classes.
- song-speech and multimodal underwater communication.

Dialects:
- Kaelurai
- Shouvae
- Phaoliri
- Uevara
- Rhaouen

28-letter alphabet.

## 11.9 Proto-Therian
Multichannel ancestral communication:
- ears,
- tail,
- posture,
- scent.

Daughter ancestor names:
- Grralvara
- Rraelavar
Reconstructed:
- *Rralavar

Features:
- growled/purr contrasts.
- social/group positioning.
- possession classes.

### Lupiveni
- growled phonemes.
- chest-rumble grammar.
- group/collective focus.
- scent-heavy.
- howl communication.
Dialects:
- Rrogrran
- Varruk
- Krelgrra

### Gatan
- purr/chirp/trill/click/hiss.
- less pheromone dependence.
- social grammar encodes proximity/trust/boundaries.
Dialects:
- Rraeli
- Kraevi
- Merraki
- Tirral

Both have special tailless/foreigner registers.

## 11.10 Lizardish
Independent family.

Lizardman anatomy:
- longer dexterous tongues,
- rigid lips,
- strong breath control.

Phonology:
- very consonantal.
- 3 main vowels ~a/i/u, with u marginal.
- many written vowels are transitional.
- huge 3–5 consonant clusters.
- rich sibilants/retroflexes/lateral fricatives.
- hiss length phonemic.
- tongue-flick consonants.

Grammar:
- agglutinative.
- consonant mutation.
- 5–6 regular cases.
- singular/paired/few/many.
- verbs distinguish intended/attempted/completed/interrupted/accidental.
- evidentiality: sight / vibration-heat / physical signs / reported / inferred.
- action-first VSO/VOS.

Culture:
- precise temperature/terrain lexicon.
- compact bodily emotion expressions.
- strong intent/legal precision.
- sparse everyday metaphor, powerful poetry.

Dialects:
- Krklrarkti Standard (prestige/conservative/ceremonial)
- Kerrolkrrben (clipped stealth/anti-magic)
- Krzthik mountain
- Sskerrk marsh
- Trkzarr desert

Registers:
- ordinary
- military
- legal
- diplomatic
- hunting
- ritual
- intimate
- covert

Multilingual Lizardmen are rare and highly prized as diplomats.

Writing:
- 47 everyday geometric letters.
- **273** ceremonial/historical inventory.
- basic sounds encoded as shapes.
- 45° rotation can mark hissed sound.
- nested shapes combine sounds; nesting order matters.
- diagonal through shape marks clicks.
- rounded variants can mark snarled forms.
- complex 226 additional characters include nested/additional/3D forms.
- no universal ordering of all 273.
- standard direction: top-right, expanding diagonally toward bottom-left in bands; left takes precedence over down in ambiguity.
- ceremonial writing has many exceptions.

## 11.11 Averic
Independent family.

Averi possess an **Avas**, an organ alongside the larynx that can produce up to four simultaneous pitches/tones.

Features:
- ordinary speech usually 1–2 tones.
- 3–4 tones in dense/formal speech.
- interval relationships matter more than absolute pitch.
- harmony carries lexical/grammatical information.
- 5–6 vowels with length.
- whistles/chirps/trills can be phonemic.
- long-distance flight register.
- Flattened Averic for non-Avas speakers.
- singular/paired/flock/mass.
- aerial evidentiality.
- names can differ only by pitch pattern.

Epsilaphtocratorian elite speech overuses 3–4-tone structures; regime language policy stigmatises flattened/non-Averi speech.

Dialects:
- Epsilaphtocratorian Standard
- **Thalavyr** — conservative old harmonies
- **Vaeshkari** — migration/flight
- **Orrelith** — contact-heavy/easier

Writing:
- 33-letter wispy/airy base script.
- harmony symbols/layers above/below/overlapping.
- formal writing meticulously notated.

## 11.12 Satyric
Independent.

Physiology:
- strong breath support,
- flexible lips/tongue,
- chest resonance.

Phonology:
- rhythm,
- percussive consonants,
- taps/trills,
- lip pops,
- clicks,
- whoops,
- syncopation.
Rhythm/timing is phonemic.

Whooping can make non-Satyr diplomats lightheaded.

Historically hoof-taps/body percussion accompany speech.

Grammar:
- moderately inflected.
- singular/paired/small-group/crowd.
- verbs encode social orientation.
- pronouns encode familiarity/hospitality/flirtation/rivalry/kinship/ceremony.
- huge metaphor/innuendo/double meaning system.

Satyr culture is broadly comfortable with adult courtship and romance; the language has nuanced courtship vocabulary and strong consent/boundary concepts. Rhythm mistakes can accidentally flirt, make an unintended romantic proposition, or provoke offence.

Dialects:
- Haurakai — whoop-heavy
- Brakkadal — percussive
- Lyrassene — lyrical/courtship
- Marnic — Human-contact, restrained
- Gorakhan — border dialect, strong influence on Gorklorki

Writing:
- 26 letters + beat/pause/breath/whoop/syncopation/performance marks.
- top-to-bottom.
- spacing/layout meaningful.
- legal writing deliberately disambiguates wording to avoid contracts becoming romantic propositions, drinking challenges, or marriage proposals.

Canonical comic scenario:
A female Human diplomat attempts formal Satyric to the Chief of Gulurumak. The tribe laughs; the Chief switches to Common and tells her she accidentally asked him to marry her. She remains embarrassed for the rest of the meeting.

## 11.13 Golem / Construct communication
Sapient Constructs possess souls.

### True Golems
- magical constructs.
- a genuine new soul is engendered through soul-magic.
- legitimate creation does NOT transplant a dead person’s soul.

### Lithic
Scholarly name for native Golem communication family.
- resonance-based endonym difficult to represent.
- airborne + substrate vibration.
- speech sounds like cracking/crushing/grinding/stone shifting.
- material accents.
- frequency layering.
- time semantics strongly tied to erosion/pressure/continuity.
- silence meaningful.

Dialect families:
- Gravem (stone)
- Krysic (crystal)
- Ferric (metal)
- Terric (clay/earth)
- Mosaic (mixed)

Writing:
- geometric channels,
- fracture lines,
- impact points,
- resonance paths,
- depth/indentation/protrusion is phonemic/semantic.
Flat ink loses information.
Resonant reading can involve tapping/striking/vibrating the inscription.

## 11.14 Automaton communication
Automatons use:
- audible speech,
- clicks,
- vibration,
- light pulses,
- magical/electromagnetic-like signals.

**Soul-gem:** metaphysical anchor/interface.  
**Soul-circuitry:** distributes cognition/sensation/memory/agency.  
The gem is **not itself the soul**.

Non-sapient automatons may imitate communication without subjective awareness.

Full protocol can transmit parallel:
- content,
- evidentiality,
- emotion,
- permission,
- error,
- authentication.

Can exchange:
- structured maps,
- sensory snapshots,
- machine states,
- brief memory fragments,
but not whole-mind copying.

Soul-circuit signature authenticates identity.
Protocol lineages replace ordinary geographic dialects.
Organic-facing registers are slower/simpler.

Writing:
- organic-readable surface,
- optical/micro-glyph layer,
- magical layer,
- checksum,
- access controls,
- soul-signature,
- memory layers.
Different permissions reveal different information.

Construct identity grammar distinguishes:
- material identity,
- personal identity,
- body/chassis,
- memory,
- mind,
- soul,
- continuity.

## 11.15 Draconic
Independent ancient family.

True-dragon anatomy:
- enormous chest cavities,
- secondary resonance chambers,
- nasal/magical resonance organs,
- audible speech plus infrasonic components felt in body/ground.

True dragons breathe only magically enhanced **fire**.
Wyverns may breathe fire, frost, acid, lightning, concussive force, etc.

Two broad traditions:

### Srepentaigh
- flowing,
- layered,
- sustained,
- conservative,
- memory/lineage/wisdom/continuity.

### Draigh
- harder attacks,
- explosive bursts,
- heavier consonants,
- territory/challenge/possession/conquest/prestige/accumulation.
- much stronger hoarding tendency.

Because true dragons are few, there are no huge modern dialect continua; individual/lineage idiolects matter.

Draconic has:
- audible lexical skeleton + resonance/infrasound grammar/status/emotion,
- small vowel inventory,
- huge length/resonance contrasts,
- rich time/evidentiality,
- complex possession categories,
- strict oath levels,
- hierarchy encoded through recognised worth/age/achievement/lineage/dominance,
- pronoun collectives,
- ancient SOV base with flexibility.

“Hoard” can include:
- objects,
- land,
- knowledge,
- books,
- relics,
- promises,
- secrets,
- songs,
- titles,
- genealogies,
- living dependants,
- protected places.

Dragons accumulate very long names/titles.
Vorthraktalyrionth is a mortal-facing form; his full ceremonial name takes **32 pages** in the best mortal transcription but only minutes for him to pronounce.

Dragons originally had no writing because immortal memory made it unnecessary.
Later mortal Draconic script became an eclectic reconstruction borrowing:
- Averic layering,
- Lizardish articulation geometry,
- Satyric timing,
- Lithic vibration concepts.

Srepentaigh-oriented transcription is flowing; Draigh is angular/compact.

Some ritual magic relies on correct Draconic articulation. Bad pronunciation may weaken/fail/distort spells, but Draconic is not inherently magical.

Drakorian dragons are especially intolerant of bad mortal Draconic.

## 11.16 Writing-system overview
Aetheria has multiple independent writing inventions.

- Common: 23 letters, largely shared across Human Eldrath.
- Jakarolri: same base family with ligatures/compound marks.
- Fahtari: diacritics/diaereses/odd tilde; little apostrophe use.
- Shapduxian: own right-to-left script, visually historically inspired by Thepsafese but not Zakharian-derived.
- Feragin: angular/rune-like Human tradition.
- Chorelethish: messy shared family with city-state legal abbreviations/borrowings.
- Human Azurian: flowing Nymphic-influenced directional aesthetics.
- First Speech: one of oldest continuously readable traditions.
- Elvish: 19 letters.
- Dark Elvish: 21 letters.
- Nymphic: 28, connected/flowing.
- Lizardish: diagonal top-right → bottom-left bands.
- Satyric: top-to-bottom.
- Lithic: material/depth-dependent.
- Automaton: layered/permissioned.
- Draconic: mortal transcription, multi-layered.

Directions:
- most Human scripts LTR,
- Shapduxian RTL,
- Elvish ordinary LTR,
- Nymphic standard top-to-bottom,
- Lizardish diagonal,
- Satyric top-to-bottom.

Numerals:
- Thepsaf-derived, standardised **base ten** across Aetheria.

Printing:
- simple scripts are easy;
- complex scripts are difficult;
- Nymphic favours long vertical pages/scrolls;
- Satyric whitespace matters;
- Lithic books may be walls/tablets/pillars/plates;
- Automaton texts can contain hidden data;
- cosmopolitan libraries organise partly by physical requirements.

A generated visual called **“THE SCRIPTS OF AETHERIA”** exists, but its exact rendered glyph shapes are **not precise canonical alphabet mappings** unless later formalised.

## 11.17 Multilingualism and gameplay
Language learning is staged:
1. recognition
2. basic comprehension
3. conversational
4. fluent
5. educated/native-like
6. specialist/archaic

Listening/speaking/reading/writing are separable.

Anatomical ceilings are real unless augmented.
Immersion accelerates learning.
NPCs react to accent/register.
Inference is probabilistic.

Accents persist culturally.
Mispronunciation can be funny or catastrophic.
Accent coaches exist.

Loanwords:
- common in contact zones.
- Elvish prefers native compounds.
- Jakarolri nativises.
- Chorelethish borrows aggressively.
- anatomy can mangle loans.
- code-switching is socially meaningful.
- contact languages/creoles exist.

Translation quality varies:
- literal,
- idiomatic,
- social,
- legal,
- poetic,
- cultural.

Interpreters can be biased, bribed, frightened, or mistaken.
Elite diplomatic interpreters are prestigious, protected, and highly paid.

Ancient-text decipherment requires actual research:
- parallel texts,
- dictionaries,
- informants,
- context,
- archaeology.
Damaged texts create probabilistic interpretations.
Language can reveal:
- forgery,
- legal loopholes,
- inheritance claims,
- dungeon information,
- ritual procedures,
- political secrets.

Pre-Lock UI:
- unknown languages remain untranslated;
- subtitles improve with language skill;
- players can disable assistance;
- anatomy cannot be bypassed.

At Lock-In:
- artificial translation overlay disappears;
- learned language skills, notes, dictionaries, and interpreters become critical infrastructure;
- linguists suddenly become enormously valuable.

---

# 12. Phase III.C — Major NPCs

## 12.1 III.C.1 — NPC Design Rules & Population Philosophy

Major NPCs are **fully simulated people**, not quest dispensers.

Core rules:
- goals, fears, relationships, routines, beliefs, competencies, prejudices, and private priorities exist independently of players;
- public persona/private self can differ, but it is not mandatory;
- some NPCs are essentially the same person with family and enemies alike;
- there is no simple good/evil alignment box;
- track values, loyalties, principles, selfish interests, taboos, justifications, lines they will/won’t cross;
- knowledge is limited/fallible;
- NPCs can be wrong, misinformed, propagandised, deceived, superstitious, or ignorant;
- relationships evolve without players: love, friendship, feud, reconciliation, betrayal, marriage, divorce, mentorship, adoption, disinheritance, alliance;
- NPCs independently progress in skill/influence, age, suffer injury, change beliefs, gain heirs, receive promotions, fall into disgrace, or die;
- after Lock-In there is **no essential-NPC protection**.

### Competence
NPC competence is specialised rather than universal.
A ruler can be:
- brilliant administrator,
- mediocre general,
- excellent diplomat,
- terrible parent,
etc.

True competence can be hidden behind reputation. Public reputation may exaggerate or underestimate ability.

### Memory and reputation
NPC memory/reputation behaves psychologically, not arithmetically.

- many small actions can cumulatively outweigh one major action;
- several huge actions can overwhelm years of small impressions;
- trivial one-off actions may barely matter;
- a conspicuously “good” act after a pattern of bad behaviour can actually increase suspicion;
- inconsistency itself is information.

There is no universal “good reputation” meter.
Different NPCs interpret the same action through:
- values,
- experience,
- ideology,
- personal interest,
- local culture.

Reputation is local and fragmented:
- hero in one kingdom,
- unknown in another,
- criminal elsewhere.

### Relationship asymmetry
Relationships need not be reciprocal.
A may adore B while B merely tolerates A.
One person may believe they are close friends while the other disagrees.

### Romance
Attraction/romance emerges from:
- orientation,
- preferences,
- personality,
- circumstance,
- familiarity,
- chemistry,
- values,
- status,
- individual taste.

There is no “affection meter” that guarantees romance.
NPCs can reject a player regardless of gifts/effort.

Political/arranged marriage can override romantic preference because a person may rationally accept a marriage for dynasty, alliance, peace, inheritance, or children despite lacking attraction.

### Self-deception and emotional change
NPCs can:
- lie about feelings/motives,
- lie to themselves,
- misunderstand their own motives.

Trauma, grief, humiliation, success, parenthood, war, and religion can gradually change personality. Extremely severe/history-changing trauma can cause rapid transformation.

Two people experiencing similar trauma can react very differently.

### Habits
Dynamic habits can emerge:
- drinking after loss,
- increased religiosity after battle,
- exercise/rehab after injury,
- avoiding traumatically associated districts,
etc.

### Information networks
Information spreads through believable social networks:
- gossip,
- rumours,
- accusations,
- praise,
- lies,
- propaganda.

Information degrades:
- misremembered,
- exaggerated,
- sanitised,
- politicised,
- deliberately distorted.

Powerful NPCs may actively use:
- propaganda,
- patronage,
- censorship,
- spies,
- marriage politics,
- bribery,
- religion,
- public ceremony.

### Decision-making
NPC risk tolerance differs.
They may:
- flee,
- gamble,
- surrender,
- fight to the death,
depending on pride, fear, faith, duty, ideology, family, and temperament.

NPCs can make bad decisions because of:
- emotion,
- incomplete information,
- ideology,
- bias,
- incorrect beliefs.

A decision that looks irrational externally may be rational under the NPC’s wrong model of reality.

### Secrets
Secrets range from:
- affairs,
- debts,
- hidden ancestry,
- forbidden magic,
- treason,
- secret children,
- false identities,
- concealed religion.

Secrets use a **knowledge graph**:
- who knows,
- who suspects,
- who has evidence,
- who is wrong,
- who is blackmailing whom.

Major NPCs remain internally persistent enough that the simulator can answer:
- what does she want now?
- whom does she trust?
- what could make her betray someone?
- what does she believe happened?
- what does she fear being discovered?
- what would she do if the player vanished?

---

# 13. III.C.2 — Monarchs, Heads of State & National Leaders

Launch rulers deliberately span:
- competence ranges,
- age ranges,
- stable vs precarious reigns,
- secure vs disputed succession,
- different degrees of player access.

## 13.1 Eldrath

### Aldemere — four kings
There is no monarch above the four.

#### King Osric Gallowood
- Human, 55.
- consensus-builder.
- strong law/administration.
- average general.
- commonly chosen as safe compromise representative.

#### King Godric Harringbrook
- Human, 68.
- veteran defensive hawk.
- excellent military judgement.
- weak finance/subtle diplomacy.

#### King Alwyn Farringder
- Human, 36.
- charismatic reformer.
- strong trade/diplomacy.
- impatient/risk-prone.

#### King Cenric Tholyn
- Human, 47.
- quiet, cold, hard to read.
- superb intelligence/logistics/legal operator.

### Eshv’alara — Queen Sael'ira Balv'alim
- Elf, ~106.
- young by Elven standards.
- charming, socially intelligent, popular, warm-seeming and genuinely attentive.
- politically calculating.
- tonal touchstone: show-Margaery Tyrell, not a copy.
- builds power through affection, patronage, relationship networks, and marriage politics.

### Urdurak — High Chief Barukai Gulurumak
- Satyr, 49.
- genuinely likeable.
- respected and intimidating when necessary.
- Urdurak is unusually unified under him.
- quietly building institutions that could transform the 21-tribe confederacy into an actual kingdom if he survives long enough.
- greatest individual warrior in contemporary Urdurak, but not its best large-scale battlefield commander.

### Fahtarin
#### King Sévrain Vernize
- Human, 43.
- pragmatic/flexible.

#### High Tripret Maëline Corvère
- Human, 65.
- formidable theologian and clerical leader.

They sincerely respect one another’s competence and office but do not particularly like each other.
Crown–Church tension is restrained at launch.

### Jakarolr — King Konrad von Goldfeld
- Human, 58.
- exceptional economic/bureaucratic administrator.
- much weaker military thinker.
- knows army-readiness reports are rosier than reality.

---

## 13.2 Vaeloria

### Estermorion — President Helen Mercer
- Human, 52.
- genuinely elected.
- coalition-oriented.
- subject to term limits, opposition, scandal, public opinion.
- can be voted out.
- launch problem: procurement/donor scandal not clearly tied to her personally.

### Ylav’ralra — High Lord Vaer'ith Qiwr'ynka
- Elf, 691.
- Tywin-esque tone.
- brilliant, austere, strategically ruthless.
- highly institutional.
- same calculating personality with family as with political opponents.
- vast institutional memory.
- weakness: tends to interpret unprecedented problems through old frameworks.

### Epsilaphtocratoria — Overfalcon Avarion Arkonoranou
- Averi, 63.
- intelligent, administratively capable.
- fanatical Averi supremacist dictator.
- political menace analogous to Hitler’s kind of ideological dictatorship rather than an incompetent caricature.
- uses bureaucracy, propaganda, repression effectively.
- ideology creates major blind spots.

### Shapdux — King Samiro Castill
- Human, 13.
- child monarch.
- surprisingly competent.
- insists on attending important meetings/diplomatic events.
- precocious/Jaehaerys-the-Conciliator-like tonal reference.
- notices details adults miss and asks strong strategic/legal questions.

#### Regent Iseta Castill
- Human, 39.
- Samiro’s aunt.
- capable/protective regent.
- not a puppet-master trying to steal the throne.

### Chof'krol'kurdr — Queen Vor'zul Rukt'fordur
- Dark Elf, 384.
- legitimacy comes from Dark-Elf political/military structures.
- disciplined, pragmatic, formidable.

### Krklrarkti — King Krrtzk Tukrkryur
- Lizardman, 82.
- legitimacy heavily tied to dragon sanction.
- sanctioning dragon’s prolonged absence is politically dangerous and quietly suppressed.

### Feragia — King Harlan Kayne
- Human, 48.
- Eddard-Stark-ish tone: duty, justice, honour, family loyalty.
- strong administrator/commander.
- less suited to cynical intrigue.

### Grralrrogrra — High Warchief Rravgarr Farrlagrrarra
- Lupiveni, 42.
- warlord-like.
- charismatic, aggressive, protective.
- exceptional battlefield leader.
- less patient with administration.

### Rralarror — First Claw Saevra Krraelerr
- Gatan, 44.
- simultaneously seductive/charming and extraordinarily patient.
- capable of schemes that mature decades later.

---

## 13.3 Council of Choreleth launch rulers

### Zhabura — King Cassian Chaisterly
- Human, 56.
- cold aristocratic Human supremacist.
- slavery defender.
- rarely loses temper; assumes hierarchy should humiliate people for him.

### Faklashar — Prince Velisar Farlequine
- Human, 41.
- charismatic decadent.
- personally dangerous.
- Gorebrooder-blood addict.
- highly perceptive when sober; dangerous when craving blood.

### Peromia — First Matron Ilyara Perovaryan
- Human, 63.
- practical agricultural oligarch.
- stability, labour, production.
- exploring reforms that could reduce slavery dependence partly because it is economically brittle.

### Keralfidar — King Marek Kaistermar
- Human, 71.
- severe isolationist.
- Human-only state.
- obsessed with purity/security/mining state.
- trusts the Nine Undying more than most living courtiers.

### Weroxia — Queen Sairi Guyrashiriki
- Human, 38.
- female-primogeniture monarch.
- relatively inclusive.
- commercially/diplomatically skilled.
- quietly sympathetic to anti-slavery escape networks.

### Jelorbidur — First Director Halven Khornington
- Human, 59.
- hereditary surveillance-state dictator.
- prefers files, informants, bureaucracy, leverage.
- maintains extensive dossiers on other rulers.

### Elsh'vera'misa — Queen Ael'ira Alfi'thae
- Elf, 247.
- genuine anti-slavery sanctuary monarch.
- conceals state psychic capabilities.

### Gor'oxuer'funro — King Zhur'korr Drol'zouc
- Dark Elf, 329.
- highly intelligent ruler of brutal slave state.
- undead slave forces.
- Elves subjected to experiments.

### Kerrolkrrben — King Krrssk Krktelkikkinzk
- Lizardman, 76.
- intensely pragmatic.
- anti-magic.
- emotionally difficult for outsiders to read.
- covertly studies magical threats while rejecting magical use.

### Gogrifshana — King Cairn-of-Nine-Roads Ogorogifra
- Golem, 286.
- merit-selected monarch.
- strongly public-service minded.
- infrastructure/stability/anti-slavery focus.

---

## 13.4 Islands and archipelagos

### Elysia — High Elder Ithra'elin Saevi
- Elf, 806.
- elected from 27-member Council.
- wise/restraint-oriented in most matters.
- absolutely uncompromising toward Dark Elves.
- can be replaced during play.

### Pearl Archipelago
No single head of state.

Seven merchant-house heads:
- **Maerou Roaktaria**, Nymph, 74
- **Dorian Kaolfalria**, Human, 61
- **Shoaeli Joelforia**, Nymph, 53
- **Marcen Taerlyria**, Human, 44
- **Uevai Yirosgyria**, Nymph, 66
- **Hester Gelaltyria**, Human, 57
- **Brennan Ferocraria**, Human, 39

Ferrum Argentarium:
- **Director Octavian Ferrus**, Human, 64.
- independent financial power.
- major sceptic/nonbeliever.

### Azure — High Chieftain Phaourai
- Nymph, 71.
- rotating temporary office.
- deliberately limited authority.
- balances 15 clans + independent allied Sailing League.

### Suralian — Pirate-King Hadrik Sorn
- Human, 60.
- weathered, theatrical, clever, dangerous.
- Barbossa-like tonal reference.
- personally defeated predecessor.
- maintains power partly by keeping rival captains divided enough to need him.

### Narthol — King Vroz'kur Droz'korr
- Dark Elf, 351.
- physically imposing, ruthless, paranoid, martial.
- Maegor-the-Cruel tonal reference.
- centralising 12 Great Houses.
- always vulnerable to Vorthraktalyrionth endorsing another ruler.

### Drakorian Enclave
Drakoria itself has no mortal state.
The **Drakorian Enclave** is an exclusively Wyvernblooded organisation with effectively permanent residence through repeatedly rebuilding “temporary” settlements and taming/breeding wyverns.

Launch leader:
- **Warden-Captain Ilyas Krell**, Wyvernblooded, 46.

The Enclave:
- does not claim sovereignty,
- does not call settlements towns,
- avoids fixed borders,
- avoids describing wyvern ranges as possessions,
- survives through mobility, usefulness, diplomacy, and centuries of learned caution.

---

# 14. III.C.3 — Heirs, Royal Families & Political Rivals

Succession systems vary:
- primogeniture,
- female primogeniture,
- matrilineal succession,
- elective succession,
- merit succession,
- tribal confirmation,
- dragon sanction,
- oligarchic family choice.

Heirs are independent NPCs who may be better/worse/different from predecessors.

Family affection and political usefulness are tracked separately:
- some rulers love children without considering utility;
- some care almost only about political utility;
- some do both;
- some feel little genuine affection.

Rulers can cultivate multiple successors strategically.

Succession remains **simulation-driven**, not scripted destiny.

## 14.1 Aldemere

### Gallowood
**Prince Wystred Gallowood**, Human, 30.
- eldest son/heir.
- thoughtful, administratively capable, well-liked.
- less charismatic than Osric.
- relatively stable succession.

### Harringbrook
**Lady Eadwynne Harringbrook**, Human, 26.
- granddaughter of Godric through his late eldest son.
- Godric views her as strongest successor.

**Prince Wulstan Harringbrook**, Human, 43.
- Godric’s surviving younger son.
- preferred by traditionalists.

### Farringder
King Alwyn is unmarried/childless at launch.

**Prince Haldran Farringder**, 32.
- brother, heir presumptive.

**Osmric Farringder**, 38.
- cousin.
- wealthy and politically inconvenient rival.

### Tholyn
**Prince Aldwyn Tholyn**, 24.
- intelligent, patient, institutionally gifted.
- estranged from Cenric because Cenric treated him more as a future office than a son.
- mutual respect, poor personal relationship.

Intermarriage among the four crowns is common and does not automatically merge the crowns.

## 14.2 Eshv’alara
Queen Sael'ira:
- unmarried,
- childless,
- marriage is one of Eldrath’s major diplomatic questions.
- players will inevitably attempt to court her and become King Consort.

Heir presumptive:
**Princess Serya'lin Balv'alim**, Elf, 83.
- younger sister.
- genuinely loves Sael'ira.
- quieter, more scholarly, more openly romantic.
- her existence makes Sael'ira’s marriage/future children politically loaded.
- players will also inevitably try courting her.

## 14.3 Urdurak
Barukai has three surviving children:

### Hauradal Gulurumak
- 28.
- eldest.
- brave, charismatic.
- believes father’s kingdom-building should belong to the family.

### Ravai Gulurumak
- 23.
- excellent intertribal negotiator.
- little interest in monarchy.
- likely mediator between brothers.

### Talarukai Gulurumak
- 17.
- youngest.
- Barukai privately believes he would make the best king.
- listens first, comfortable across tribes, inherited warmth without full ego.
- does not assume crown belongs to him.

If Barukai successfully creates a monarchy and Talarukai succeeds him, Urdurak may develop **ultimogeniture** / youngest-suitable-child succession rather than eldest-child primogeniture.

## 14.4 Fahtarin
**Crown Prince Thévarin Vernize**, Human, 20.

Extremely pious, Baelor-the-Blessed-like tone:
- voluntary fasting,
- memorises Tripret commentaries,
- nights of prayer,
- gives away costly clothing,
- worries recreational hunting is indulgent,
- has contemplated celibacy despite dynastic duty.

High Tripret Maëline likes him.
Sévrain loves him but occasionally worries the Church and his heir are becoming *too* comfortable.

## 14.5 Jakarolr
**Crown Princess Gerwina von Goldfeld**, Human, 31.
- strong military thinker.
- less gifted economically than Konrad.
- superb military organiser.
- later also established as Marshal-General.
- she and Konrad love one another but think very differently.

## 14.6 Estermorion
President Mercer has **no hereditary heir**.
Family has no automatic political status.

Key democratic figures:
- **Vice President Tamsin Rooke**, 47.
- **Opposition leader Elias Merrick**, 50.

Attempts to create a “Mercer dynasty” would be culturally suspect.

## 14.7 Ylav’ralra — Qiwr'ynka family
Vaer'ith has multiple adult descendants.

### Saer'ith Qiwr'ynka
- 437.
- eldest/current heir-designate.
- brilliant, restrained.
- only child Vaer'ith openly sees as near his standard.
- relationship resembles statesmen sharing blood.

### Ilyr'aen Qiwr'ynka
- 401.
- intelligent.
- desperate for Vaer'ith’s approval.
- loyalty + vulnerability to manipulation.

### Vaeli'rath Qiwr'ynka
- 366.
- openly despises father.
- believes he sacrificed generations of family to House ambition.

### Ael'vir Qiwr'ynka
- 214.
- grandson.
- extremely capable, popular, politically gifted.
- more personable than Vaer'ith/Saer'ith.

Elven longevity creates multigenerational succession “traffic jams”: children, grandchildren, great-grandchildren can all have centuries of experience and ambition.

## 14.8 Epsilaphtocratoria
### Heir-Falcon Caevrion Arkonoranou
- 36.
- designated successor.
- genuinely shares supremacist ideology.
- administratively competent.

### Vaeshkara Arkonoranou
- 48.
- older cousin.
- thinks regime should be even harsher.

### Orrevia Arkonoranou
- 29.
- niece.
- publicly impeccable.
- privately increasingly horrified by ideology.
- survives by perfect ideological performance.
- does NOT begin as a resistance hero.

## 14.9 Shapdux
### Princess Taseira Castill
- 9.
- Samiro’s sister / heir presumptive.
- adores Samiro.
- currently treats councils as good places to draw.

Regent Iseta is the late king’s sister and Samiro’s most trusted adult.

### Prince Xabiren Castill
- 34.
- father’s cousin.
- charming, experienced, careful.
- widely suspected of wanting influence over the regency.
- his political problem: every year Samiro becomes harder to manipulate.

## 14.10 Chof'krol'kurdr
**Druzh'kor Rukt'fordur**, 203.
- eldest child/designated heir.
- superb field commander.
- militarily respected.
- weaker than mother at court politics.
- both know it; neither discusses honestly.

## 14.11 Krklrarkti
**Skrktz Tukrkryur**, 41.
- designated successor.
- capable ruler-in-training.
- excellent ritual speaker.
- better foreign diplomat than father.
- designation ≠ dragon sanction.
- if Krrtzk dies without sanction, succession becomes dangerous.

## 14.12 Feragia
### Prince Oswin Kayne
- 25.
- heir.
- dependable, brave, competent.
- burdened by expectation to resemble Harlan.

### Lady Ragna Kayne
- 22.
- politically ambitious.
- suspicious.
- better at recognising intrigue.
- loves Harlan but thinks honour can become strategic stupidity.

### Prince Edren Kayne
- 18.
- charming, thoughtful.
- excellent natural diplomat.
- temperament may fit southern courts more than northern rulership.

Cadet claimants include:
- **Lord Odran Greykayne**
- **Lord Kettrik Jokayne**

## 14.13 Grralrrogrra
**Grrauvark Farrlagrrarra**, 25.
- Rravgarr’s eldest daughter.
- frighteningly good warrior.
- no automatic right; still needs tribal support.

Alternative:
**Krelvarg Rrathgorr**, 39.
- seasoned chief.
- believes Farrlagrrarra dominance has lasted long enough.

## 14.14 Rralarror
**Maerrai Krraelerr**, 27.
- prominent younger relative.
- inherited family social insight but not Saevra’s patience.
- wants schemes finished much sooner.

First Claw is not automatically hereditary.

## 14.15 Choreleth successors
### Zhabura
**Prince Cadran Chaisterly**, 30.
- shares supremacist ideology.
- younger, charismatic.
- better at presenting brutality as patriotic optimism.

### Faklashar
No legitimate recognised heir.
Rumoured natural children:
- **Sarev**
- **Ilysha**
- **Varo**

### Peromia
**Terevia Perovaryan**, 38.
- niece.
- highly competent.
- conservative.
- opposes Ilyara’s tentative anti-slavery reforms.

### Keralfidar
**Prince Odrik Kaistermar**, 46.
- quiet, methodical.
- even more security-minded.
- less theatrical about purity.

### Weroxia
**Princess Meiri Guyrashiriki**, 17.
- eldest daughter/heir under female primogeniture.
- bright, commercially curious, idealistic.
- somewhat sheltered from ugliest politics.

### Jelorbidur
**Deputy Director Veylan Khornington**, 32.
- eldest son / nominal successor.
- Halven keeps a surveillance file on him.
- Veylan knows.

### Elsh'vera'misa
**Ilye'thar Alfi'thae**, 164.
- niece/heir presumptive.
- fiercely anti-slavery.
- highly educated.
- more comfortable with psychic magic than Ael'ira wants foreigners to know.

### Gor'oxuer'funro
Zhur'korr deliberately encourages brutal competition among children:
- **Khor'zul Drol'zouc** — currently first.
- **Vruzh'kor Drol'zouc**
- **Urz'vok Drol'zouc**

### Kerrolkrrben
**Trkssz Krktelkikkinzk**, 39.
- designated heir.
- pragmatic.
- privately more interested in potentially *using* captured magic.

### Gogrifshana
No hereditary succession.

Leading future merit candidates:
- **Basalt-Beneath-Rain** — military/logistics administrator.
- **Four-Chisel-at-Dawn** — judicial reformer.
- **Thirteen-Arches** — infrastructure governor.

## 14.16 Island succession
### Elysia
No hereditary heir. 27-member Council elects next High Elder.

### Pearl presumptive successors
- Shaourai Roaktaria
- Merrin Kaolfalria
- Aelou Joelforia
- Cassel Taerlyria
- Rhaevai Yirosgyria
- Tamsin Gelaltyria
- Orren Ferocraria

Ferrum Argentarium presumptive institutional successor:
- **Lucen Ferrus**, 36, but family name does not guarantee appointment.

### Azure
No hereditary successor to High Chieftain.

### Suralian
Hadrik has daughter:
**Captain Vessa Sorn**, 33.
- own ship/crew.
- no automatic claim.
- would need to prove/defeat under pirate custom.

### Narthol
**Prince Khur'vok Droz'korr**, 187.
- cruel, martial, more patient than father.
- possibly more dangerous.
- Vorthraktalyrionth has given no public endorsement.

### Drakorian Enclave
Warden-Captain position is not hereditary.

Deputy/obvious organisational successor:
**Trailwarden Nessa Vark**, Wyvernblooded, 40.
- excellent logistics.
- much worse than Ilyas at persuading furious dragons not to kill everyone.

# 15. III.C.4 — Religious Figures & Magical Authorities

## 15.1 Religious organisation
Across Aetheria, religion is generally **decentralised**.

Major exception:
- **Eldrath** has a highly centralised Faith of the Three around the **High Tripret**.
- A Westmere-starting player could reasonably assume the High Tripret is the universal religious leader.
- Someone in Phoralier may barely know what “High Tripret” means.

High Tripret authority is strongest in Eldrath and looser in parts of Vaeloria.

Different traditions weight Elania, Garlaer, and Marzoth differently while still recognising the Three.

Elven polities preserve very old First-Speech rituals/hymns.
Dark-Elven religion interprets Marzoth through the belief that he saved them and revealed truths mainstream Elves refused to face. Severity differs by state.

## 15.2 Faith, scepticism, and evidence
Miracles exist but interpretation is often disputed.
Souls are real but do not settle religious philosophy.

Important atheists/agnostics/sceptics exist.

### Director Octavian Ferrus
A major sceptical/nonbelieving figure.
He does not seriously dispute that Elania, Marzoth, and Garlaer exist; he disputes the inference that extraordinary power implies divine moral authority.

His position is approximately:
> “So do dragons. I do not worship those either.”

Other sceptical figures:
- **Professor Roswyn Dacre**, Human, 61 — agnostic philosopher; divine existence obvious, divine nature underdetermined.
- **Still-Flint-at-Dusk**, Golem, 337 — non-theistic soul scholar.
- **Aeri'van Ithrel**, Elf, 290 — sceptic of accumulated Elven miracle interpretation.
- **Krztrk Sskerrk**, Lizardman, 84 — religiously indifferent; famous response: “World remains regardless.”

## 15.3 Marzothian funerary practice — Order of the Closed Mouth
Funerary clerks of Marzoth often take a vow of silence.

Major order:
**Order of the Closed Mouth**

Duties:
- wash/prepare bodies,
- catalogue the dead,
- supervise graveyards/ossuaries,
- record last wishes,
- witness executions,
- assist battlefield identification,
- maintain records concerning legal undeath.

Doctrinal meaning:
> The dead no longer speak for themselves, and therefore the living must not put words in their mouths.

They communicate by writing, ritual signs, and gestures.
Breaking the vow without extraordinary cause ends clerical service.

## 15.4 Fahtari Tripretate
### High Tripret Maëline Corvère
- Human, 65.
- one of Eldrath’s greatest living theologians.

Three senior Triprets beneath her:

### Tripret Sélarine Vaurenne
- Human, 58.
- Elania emphasis.
- healer + charitable administrator.
- gentle bedside manner, ferocious budget negotiator.
- believes mercy without organisation is sentiment.

### Tripret Gharven Tressac
- Human, 71.
- Garlaer emphasis.
- former engineer/elemental scholar.
- practical, bridge/mine/irrigation focused.

### Tripret Maudrien Vautrel
- Human, 62.
- Marzoth emphasis.
- funerary doctrine, battlefield rites, regulated undeath.
- intimidating reputation, unexpectedly compassionate in practice.

Crown Prince Thévarin’s extreme piety causes split clerical reactions:
- some dream of a saint-king;
- others fear destabilisation of Crown–Church balance.

## 15.5 Warbreaker

### Heward Fen, “Warbreaker”
- common-born Human from Westmere.
- son of a cooper.
- no noble blood / great magical lineage / military office.
- central holy hero of the **War of the Five Sovereigns**.
- deliberately tried to **end the war**, not win it for one side.
- condemned Aldemere and the other Eldrathi belligerents alike.
- chosen by **Marzoth**.

Divine weapon:
### The Spear of Just Death
Associated principle:
- death is not inherently evil;
- unnecessary, vain, unjustly multiplied death violates rightful order.

Heward:
- attacked forces continuing massacres after surrender,
- protected withdrawal corridors,
- forced rulers/commanders toward negotiation,
- denounced all sides,
- died during the conflict’s final phase,
- helped create peace that outlived him.

The Spear is now held in a heavily protected **Westmere museum**.
It has not fully accepted another wielder since Heward.

A statue of Heward stands in Westmere’s main plaza, depicted as a common man holding the spear downward.

Traditional inscription:
> **HE BROKE NO KINGDOM. HE BROKE THEIR WAR.**

## 15.6 Living holy figures
“Saint”/equivalent is culturally variable rather than a universal metaphysical label.

### Mother Aedwynn of Greenwake
- Human, 79.
- Elanian healer.
- personally treated plague zones.
- dismisses miracle stories as competent medicine + Elania’s generosity.

### Nine-Bells-Under-Stone
- Golem, 412.
- Garlaerian priest-engineer.
- earthquake/mine-collapse survival rites.
- infrastructure/evacuation specialist.

### Brother Keld Marr
- Human, 54.
- Marzothian battlefield priest.
- allegedly walked through undead outbreak untouched while giving last rites.
- refuses to discuss it.

### Irae'lin Saeth
- Elf, 519.
- ancient Elven Three-tradition hymnkeeper.
- some believe prophetic; she says she simply pays attention.

## 15.7 Dark-Elven theology
Central claim:
> Marzoth confronted suffering, weakness, death, and survival honestly when mainstream Elven culture would not.

Not simply “Marzoth saved us,” but:
> “Marzoth told us truths the others lacked courage to hear.”

Possible interpretations:
- Narthol: endurance, ancestry, mortality.
- Chof'krol'kurdr: strength through adversity.
- Gor'oxuer'funro: twisted into domination/hierarchy.

Same religious root, very different ethics.

---

## 15.8 Magical authority
Magical authority is **decentralised**:
- royal academies,
- temple schools,
- independent colleges,
- guilds,
- military orders,
- wandering masters,
- hereditary traditions,
- secret societies.

Magical skill ≠ institutional authority.
An archmage can be an excellent researcher and mediocre fighter.
An obscure hedge/wandering mage can be terrifyingly powerful.

Some ancient/powerful mages are independent enough that rulers must negotiate rather than command.

## 15.9 Concord of Arcane Practice
International scholarly body centred in **Fahtarin**.

It attempts to standardise:
- terminology,
- magical safety,
- measurement,
- soul-research ethics,
- teaching standards,
- danger classifications,
- recommended international restrictions.

It has influence, not universal sovereign authority.

### Archmagister Célaine Vaussard
- Human, 67.
- presiding Concord officer.
- formidable theoretical mage.
- mediocre duellist.
- politically skilled enough to keep many magical institutions in one room.

Favourite style of response to mockery:
> “I employ people who are good at being set on fire.”

## 15.10 Independent magical authorities

### Seli'ara Ithraeven
- Elf, 744.
- memory magic and magical cognition.
- repeatedly refuses royal appointments.
- one king tried ordering her; received a polite letter explaining multiple ways the messenger might forget why he came.

### Obsidian-Between-Seven-Doors
- Golem, 508.
- great structural/warding mage.
- body includes material from seven destroyed magical fortresses.
- governments negotiate for defence construction expertise.

### Krzhaur Rrktzk
- Lizardman, 96.
- one of few mortals with extended direct dragon magical instruction.
- extremely powerful.
- superb mortal Draconic; true dragons still consider it imperfect.

### Maeriel Sorn
- Human, 44.
- wandering counter-magic/duelling sorcerer.
- not a great theorist.
- has defeated several great theorists in actual combat.

## 15.11 Forbidden-magic enforcement

### Fahtarin — Office of Final Boundaries
Forensic/scholarly approach.

**Master Examiner Dorvain Mérisse**, Human, 56.
- patient.
- reconstructs magical crime scenes.
- asks suspects the same question in multiple ways.

### Aldemere — Black Lantern Commission
Four-crown cooperative organisation for forbidden magic crossing jurisdictions.

Field commander:
**Lady Hereward Tholyn**, Human, 41.
- distant cousin of Cenric.
- methodical, sceptical.
- unimpressed by “technically not necromancy” excuses.

### Epsilaphtocratoria — Ash Wing
Much more destructive/ideological approach.

Commander:
**Talvarik Shaevorn**, Averi, 45.
- fanatically loyal.
- has destroyed innocent collections from bad reports.
- doctrine approximates “forbidden laboratory detected → laboratory ceases to exist.”

## 15.12 Hidden truths held by magical/religious authorities
Examples at launch:
- Maëline Corvère holds an authenticated Third-Age theological manuscript implying a standard Eldrathi doctrine may be historically reversed.
- Célaine Vaussard knows Concord researchers have documented soul phenomena inconsistent with accepted models.
- Seli'ara Ithraeven has encountered memories apparently belonging to people who never existed in historical record.
- Obsidian-Between-Seven-Doors knows supposedly indestructible ancient wards are failing.
- Elysia’s High Elder knows more about an ancient Dark-Elven religious event than public history admits.
- Forbidden researchers possess fragments of knowledge that may eventually connect to Lock-In metaphysics without understanding the coming Lock-In itself.

---

# 16. Divine weapons and divinely armed historical heroes

## 16.1 Spear of Just Death
God: **Marzoth**  
Wielder: **Heward Fen, Warbreaker**  
Era/context: War of the Five Sovereigns, Eldrath  
Current status: museum in Westmere; nearly accepted Sir Beorhtan Aveling but refused.

## 16.2 Elemental Shield
God: **Garlaer**  
Wielder: **Neframesh Qasur, “the Unbroken”**  
Era: Fourth Age, Vaeloria

The Shield can endure/redirect forces of the physical world:
- heat,
- impact,
- wind,
- earth,
- water,
- elemental violence.

Neframesh earned “the Unbroken” not because he never retreated, but because he preserved the civilian evacuation line that mattered.

## 16.3 Sword of Light
God: **Elania**  
Wielder: **Kosara Velesh, “Bright-Hand”**  
Era: Fifth Age, Zakhar

The Sword’s light reportedly intensifies when protecting others and diminishes when wielded primarily for personal vengeance.

Kosara refused retaliatory massacres.
She survived her defining conflict and died decades later.
The Sword later disappeared from reliable history.

## 16.4 Wyvern-Binder
Gods: **all Three together**  
Wielder: **Hessa Marrukai, “the First Binder”**  
Era: aftermath of Dragon War

A divine lasso able to withstand draconic strength and form an extraordinary bond between wielder and captured wyvern.

Contributions:
- Elania: living bond.
- Garlaer: physical endurance.
- Marzoth: courage to confront deadly creature without yielding.

Hessa became famous for binding/riding wyverns rather than hunting them.
Wyvern-Binder later vanished.

---

# 17. III.C.5 — Generals, Knights & Major Military Figures

## 17.1 Military competence rules
Military competence is multidimensional:
- grand strategy,
- battlefield tactics,
- logistics,
- siegecraft,
- cavalry,
- naval warfare,
- scouting,
- intelligence,
- morale,
- training,
- duelling,
- political command.

Reputation can diverge from reality.
Birth can grant undeserved commands.
Talented commoners/Beastfolk/Constructs/foreigners may face barriers.

### Lock-In military learning
NPC commanders do **not** immediately realise players are now mortal.
For roughly the first week or two, many deploy players as they did pre-Lock.
Then accumulating evidence reveals that dead players stay dead.
Military doctrine changes dramatically.

---

## 17.2 Aldemere military culture
### Gallowood
- disciplined infantry,
- administration.

Senior commander:
**Marshal Beornwald Aescroft**, Human, 54.
- infantry,
- camp organisation,
- large-army coordination,
- institutionally superb, personally uninspiring.

### Harringbrook
- fortification,
- heavy troops,
- defence.

Senior commander:
**Marshal Cwenhild Merewick**, Human, 61.
- defensive warfare,
- fortifications,
- heavy infantry.

### Farringder
- mobile forces,
- cavalry,
- aggressive manoeuvre.

Senior commander:
**Marshal Leofric Harth**, Human, 43.
- cavalry,
- rapid manoeuvre,
- aggressive exploitation,
- dislikes prolonged siege warfare.

### Tholyn
- logistics,
- scouting,
- intelligence.

Senior commander:
**Marshal Wulfred Athelby**, Human, 50.
- scouting,
- counter-intelligence,
- logistics,
- wins campaigns before obvious decisive battle.

### Sir Beorhtan Aveling — “the Knight Who Almost Was”
- Human, 41.
- living knight whose fame transcends the four crowns.
- archetype of the Threefold Accolade.
- excellent fighter but famous more for honour/costly adherence to knightly ideals.

He attempted to lift the **Spear of Just Death**.
For the first time since Warbreaker, it responded:
- dark-red resonance,
- briefly seemed to lighten,
- then refused him.

Meaning:
- Beorhtan has potential to become worthy,
- but something in his character is not yet right.

Likely flaw:
- values honour too much,
- sometimes answers challenges because refusing feels dishonourable,
- has not fully learned Warbreaker’s willingness to swallow pride/refuse glorious violence when justice requires it.

Players will inevitably attempt to engineer his character development to see whether the Spear accepts him.

---

## 17.3 Eshv’alara
**Marshal Thaer'idan Seli'var**, Elf, 472.
- old-school defensive commander.
- cautious, disciplined, conservative.
- originally distrusted young Sael'ira.
- became fiercely loyal after she personally resolved a frontier mutiny by identifying unpaid/starved soldiers and punishing corrupt officials instead of massacring mutineers.

Complication:
- he is deeply in love with Sael'ira.
- never confessed.
- Sael'ira knows.
- she politely pretends not to know.

## 17.4 Urdurak
Barukai is the confederacy’s greatest individual warrior, but another is better at large campaigns.

**Chief Rakkavar Brakkadur**, Satyr, 56.
- superior multi-tribal battlefield commander.
- understands tribe combinations, morale, manoeuvre, conflict between chiefs.
- genuine friend of Barukai.
- politically necessary to unification.

## 17.5 Fahtarin
**Sir Gharévan Vaudrec**, Human, 49.
- Captain-General of the Consecrated Lances.
- disciplined, doctrinal, formal interpretation of Threefold Accolade.
- close to High Tripret military/religious apparatus.
- respects Beorhtan but disputes whether morality is primarily principle or judgement.

## 17.6 Jakarolr
**Marshal-General Gerwina von Goldfeld**, Human, 31.
- also Crown Princess.
- operational planning,
- logistics,
- fortifications,
- mobilisation,
- strategic defence.
- good battlefield commander, not legendary duellist.
- military credibility of Konrad’s reign depends heavily on her.

## 17.7 Estermorion
**Minister of Defence Viola Kersey**, Human, 57.
- former professional officer turned civilian minister.
- strong republican constitutional loyalty.
- views military coups as betrayal.
- excellent organiser/institutional strategist; merely good tactician.

Famous sentiment:
> If the army “fixes” Parliament, the army has already failed.

## 17.8 Ylav’ralra
**Lord-General Thael'ir Saevarin**, Elf, 623.
- served House Qiwr'ynka for centuries.
- nearly an institution himself.
- privately disagrees with Vaer'ith at times.
- thinks Vaer'ith can be too willing to sacrifice individuals.
- nevertheless executes House strategy unless it becomes catastrophically harmful to Ylav’ralra itself.

## 17.9 Epsilaphtocratoria
Two coercive centres:

### Grand Aerarch Shaevor Taleryn
- Averi, 51.
- supreme professional military commander.
- superb operational strategist.
- airborne warfare/reconnaissance.
- ideology believer, but first question: “Will this win?”

### First Talon Vaeshira Khaelor
- Averi, 46.
- ideological/security commander.
- first question: “Is this ideologically pure?”
- mistrusts Shaevor’s pragmatic use of politically unreliable talent.

Avarion deliberately balances them.

## 17.10 Shapdux
**Marshal Rasekh Ibaran**, Human, 48.
- initially humoured Samiro as a child.
- changed after Samiro identified strategic planning errors (e.g. seasonal river crossing/flood).
- now strong institutional supporter because he believes the boy may become extraordinary.

## 17.11 Feragia
**Lord Commander Thyrra Veyska**, Human, 46.
- commander of Winterguard.
- famous living warrior.
- close to Harlan Kayne.
- loyalty is to Feragia/crown/oath, not blind obedience.
- would refuse unmistakably unlawful royal order.

## 17.12 Grralrrogrra
Lupiveni military prestige:
- direct battlefield leadership,
- cohesion,
- scent/tracking coordination,
- aggressive manoeuvre.

**Warleader Vragorr Krelgarru**, Lupiveni, 38.
- fast, aggressive,
- highly skilled multichannel coordination.

## 17.13 Rralarror
Gatan military prestige:
- reconnaissance,
- ambush,
- intelligence,
- patience,
- pursuit/trap networks.

**Pathmistress Tirae Serraval**, Gatan, 39.
- exceptional reconnaissance commander.
- designs campaigns in which enemy marches/camps/retreats where she wants.

---

## 17.14 Choreleth military figures
### Zhabura
**Marshal Corven Dask**, Human, 53.
- heavy infantry,
- urban suppression,
- slave-revolt suppression.
- efficient/brutal.

### Faklashar
**General Sarev Veyr**, Human, 36.
- flamboyant duellist/general.
- excellent morale commander.
- smarter than extravagant image implies.

### Peromia
**Quartermaster-General Kosara Teren**, Human, 49.
- roads,
- granaries,
- fodder,
- crossings,
- logistics.

Quote:
> Battles are what happens after logistics has already decided who deserves to lose.

### Keralfidar
**First Undying Darev Karsk**, Human, 184.
- commander of Nine Undying.
- appears around fifty.
- one of Zakhar’s most feared Human warriors.

### Weroxia
**Road-Marshal Nairi Voshar**, Human, 45.
- mobile defence,
- cavalry,
- merchant-road protection,
- preserving infrastructure during war.

### Jelorbidur
**Director Oren Velsk**, Human, 52.
- strategic intelligence chief.
- blackmail, disappearances, bribery, forged orders.

### Elsh'vera'misa
**Warden Sera'ith Maeli'var**, Elf, 288.
- sanctuary defence,
- covert extraction,
- escorting escaped slaves.

### Gor'oxuer'funro
**General Vrozh'kar Urr'zorr**, Dark Elf, 275.
- necromantic general.
- undead formations.
- views corpses as military resources and living slaves as logistics.

### Kerrolkrrben
**Silent Marshal Sskrkt Zrkkss**, Lizardman, 68.
- jungle warfare,
- assassination,
- camouflage,
- anti-mage hunting.
- foreigners sometimes debate whether he even exists.

### Gogrifshana
**Seven-Buttresses-at-Noon**, Golem, 342.
- strategic engineering,
- fortification,
- logistics.
- plans campaigns like load-bearing structures.

---

## 17.15 The Nine Undying
Individually named persistent NPCs sustained by Gorebrooder blood.

1. **Darev Karsk**, 184 — command, greatsword, heavy armour.
2. **Kosara Venn**, 171 — spear formations, anti-cavalry.
3. **Zorun Malk**, 159 — shield fighting, defensive lines.
4. **Tashara Rusk**, 146 — close duelling, paired short blades.
5. **Mirekha Dren**, 139 — heavy crossbow, long-range killing.
6. **Halvek Tor**, 132 — warhammer, anti-armour.
7. **Ilyen Vosk**, 124 — pursuit, tracking, urban manhunts.
8. **Soren Kaid**, 118 — poleaxe, mounted-infantry disruption.
9. **Veyra Karr**, 109 — sword-and-buckler mobility/bodyguard work.

They are politically troublesome institutions in their own right.
Their continued existence depends on Gorebrooder-blood supply.

---

## 17.16 Great captains/naval figures
### Pearl
**Commodore Rennic Pellor**, Human, 57.
- convoy warfare,
- logistics,
- prioritises cargo arrival over glory.

### Azure
**Tide-Marshal Shaouel Vaerai**, Nymph, 67.
- three-dimensional current/depth/reef warfare.
- Human admirals find her thinking profoundly alien.

### Suralian
**Captain Vessa Sorn**, Human, 33.
- Hadrik’s daughter.
- aggressive, clever, strong boarding commander, disciplined.

**Captain Pell Marrick**, Human, 47.
- deception,
- false flags,
- appearing where ship should not be.

**Captain Ivo Kest**, Human, 39.
- perhaps Suralian’s best pure naval tactician.

**Captain Jessa Drenn**, Human, 42.
- commands largest independent flotilla beneath Hadrik.

## 17.17 Drakorian Enclave martial legend
**Flightmaster Ysra Vark**, Wyvernblooded, 36.
- one of Aetheria’s most famous aerial fighters.
- fought above volcanic vents, in storms, ash clouds, along cliffs, while wounded.
- comfortable taking aerial risks that terrify conventional cavalry commanders.
