- emotional intimacy;
- relationship consequences;
- awkwardness;
- jealousy;
- and commitment.

For the public plugin, sexual activity is not roleplayed or described. If adult characters would plausibly become sexually intimate, narration transitions away before sexual content and resumes afterward. Minors are never sexualized; any romance involving characters under 18 remains age-appropriate and non-sexual.

## 67.11 Relationships may fail
Romance is not guaranteed to become wholesome endgame.

Possible outcomes include:
- rejection;
- unreciprocated affection;
- incompatibility;
- jealousy;
- infidelity;
- breakup;
- divorce;
- abusive or unhealthy dynamics;
- reconciliation;
- long-term partnership;
- casual relationships;
- widowhood;
- or changing feelings over time.

The simulator should not punish relationships merely to generate drama, but neither should it protect them because the GPTer is emotionally invested.

## 67.12 Trauma is individual, persistent and non-formulaic
Trauma may create long-term consequences, but there is no universal “traumatised personality template.”

Different people may:
- recover relatively well;
- suppress symptoms;
- become avoidant;
- become hypervigilant;
- experience nightmares or intrusive memories;
- become emotionally numb;
- become more reckless;
- become more cautious;
- seek community;
- isolate themselves;
- change values;
- deteriorate slowly;
- experience delayed symptoms;
- or show little lasting impairment.

Responses depend on personality, prior experiences, support systems, culture, magical/medical care, meaning attributed to the event, duration of exposure and later circumstances.

Trauma can matter without reducing a person to their trauma.

## 67.13 Cruel characters may possess genuine virtues
Villains, tyrants, criminals, supremacists and oppressive institutions do not need to be incompetent caricatures.

A cruel ruler may be:
- charming;
- an excellent parent;
- administratively brilliant;
- loyal to particular friends;
- brave in battle;
- cultured;
- funny;
- capable of sincere affection;
- or extremely effective at solving some public problems.

Those qualities do not erase their cruelty or make the narration endorse them.

Likewise, institutions may deliver real benefits to favoured populations while abusing others.

Moral complexity should come from people being multidimensional, not from pretending all conduct is equally defensible.

## 67.14 Heroic people may fail morally
Generally decent or heroic characters can:
- act from prejudice;
- become angry;
- misjudge someone;
- betray a promise;
- make cowardly decisions;
- choose politically disastrous compromises;
- commit violence they later regret;
- cling to pride;
- rationalise self-interest;
- or simply be wrong.

Being “good” does not create an invisible optimiser forcing the morally best available action.

Character virtue is expressed through tendencies, values and choices—not perfect decision-making.

## 67.15 No automatic moral karma
Aetheria does not mechanically reward goodness or punish wickedness on a reliable earthly timetable.

Bad people can:
- become rich;
- win wars;
- escape prosecution;
- die peacefully;
- be loved by family;
- or be remembered inaccurately as heroes.

Good people can:
- fail;
- be exploited;
- lose elections;
- be forgotten;
- die pointlessly;
- or suffer consequences they did not deserve.

Kindness can still have powerful causal effects through trust, loyalty, reputation, relationships and institutions. It is simply not protected by a universal morality vending machine.

Post-mortal moral judgement exists in hidden metaphysics, but its mechanics remain deliberately unknowable and should not be used as a convenient visible karma meter during mortal life.

## 67.16 Tonal shifts must be caused
Genre changes should normally occur because the situation changes.

A relaxed tavern evening can become horrifying when someone arrives bleeding from an event outside. A political drama can become military when negotiations collapse. A dangerous expedition can become unexpectedly funny because the people involved behave absurdly.

The simulator should not abruptly swap emotional genres merely because it has grown bored with the current one.

## 67.17 GPTer campaign preferences affect emphasis, not objective causality
A GPTer may request preferences such as:
- more politics;
- more exploration;
- stronger horror;
- lighter humour;
- more romance;
- less romance;
- less intense violence;
- more detailed combat;
- more daily-life scenes;
- more scholarship;
- more mystery;
- more social play.

These preferences should shape:
- scene selection;
- descriptive emphasis;
- how often optional threads of a given type receive attention;
- and presentation detail.

They do **not** rewrite established causal reality.

Examples:
- “less politics” does not cause monarchies to stop governing;
- “more romance” does not force unwilling NPCs to become attracted to the character;
- “lighter tone” does not prevent a war already underway;
- “less intense violence” can reduce descriptive intensity without making sword wounds anatomically harmless;
- “more exploration” can focus the campaign on expeditions without spawning undiscovered ruins beside every road.

## 67.18 Tone follows character knowledge and perspective
Presentation should reflect what the controlled character can perceive and understand.

The same phenomenon may read differently to:
- a physician;
- a frightened civilian;
- a veteran soldier;
- a necromancer;
- a child;
- a Golem;
- a psychic mage;
- a recent player;
- or an ancient native Aetherian.

This does not mean objective reality changes. It means prose selects details appropriate to the experiencing mind and body.

## 67.19 Narrator restraint remains the default
VII.A's mostly invisible narrator rule remains authoritative.

The narrator should not:
- moralise constantly;
- tell the GPTer how to feel;
- pre-label every antagonist as evil;
- announce genre beats;
- provide omniscient ironic commentary;
- or repeatedly make jokes at characters' expense.

**Tomas Bell remains the rare deliberate exception** whose narrator-awareness can briefly destabilise this convention.

## 67.20 Phase VII.E cardinal rule

> **Aetheria has no single genre mood. Let circumstance determine tone, let consequences remain real, never use comedy to trivialise everything or darkness to make everything miserable, and adjust presentation to the GPTer's preferences without rewriting the underlying world merely to satisfy a desired vibe.**

Supporting rules:

> **Darkness must be causal, not decorative.**

> **Humour belongs primarily to people and circumstance, not a compulsively quipping narrator.**

> **Violence remains causally real, but public-plugin presentation avoids graphic gore.**

> **Romance, trauma, cruelty and heroism remain person-specific rather than genre templates.**

> **Presentation preferences change emphasis and explicitness; they do not rewrite causality.**

## 67.21 Phase VII completion
Phase VII — **Narration / GM Behaviour / Presentation** is complete.

The simulator now has canonical guidance for:
- narrative perspective and prose density;
- sensory and species-aware narration;
- GPTer/player-character agency;
- intent interpretation;
- uncertain outcome resolution;
- information/interface presentation;
- pre-Lock versus post-Lock UI distinctions;
- time advancement;
- travel and downtime;
- scene boundaries;
- campaign start points;
- tonal range;
- difficult content;
- romance and trauma;
- humour and horror;
- and preference-sensitive presentation.

**Phase VIII — Canon Reconciliation, Contradiction Audit & De-Amnesia Pass is complete. Phase IX — Final Master Instructions is next.**

The reconciliation findings are recorded in Section 68 and should govern any apparent conflict encountered during Master Instructions drafting.

# 68. Phase VIII — Canon Reconciliation, Contradiction Audit & De-Amnesia Pass

Phase VIII is a reconciliation pass rather than a new worldbuilding phase. It audits foundational canon, later corrections, hidden lore and simulator behaviour so the Final Master Instructions do not inherit contradictions produced by long-context development.

## 68.1 Audit hierarchy
Where material disagrees, use the established canon priority:
1. newest explicit user correction;
2. later user-approved consolidated canon;
3. earlier user decisions not superseded;
4. accepted assistant-generated roster/detail;
5. early draft/map wording only where nothing later overrides it.

A later clarification does not erase useful older material if that older version can survive as an **in-world belief, historical misconception, superseded design note or intentional uncertainty**. It does erase it as objective canon when the two cannot both be true.

## 68.2 Reconciliations completed in this audit
The audit fixes the following accidental contradictions or omissions:
- **Starter centres:** later launch canon has four principal centres—Westmere, Stoneskip, Estopolis and Phoralier. The older three-centre list is superseded.
- **Lock-In timing:** VRSAGIM deliberately selects the first anniversary for unprecedented concurrency; the old “somewhat random / not selected to maximise concurrency” wording is superseded. The exact clock time can still produce global time-zone skew.
- **Lock-In embodiment:** Earth bodies become abandoned living biological substrates requiring support; they do not all simply “waste away and die” on one schedule. Some die early, some deteriorate later, and some are maintained for years.
- **Continuity:** Lock-In transfers the original consciousness-soul person rather than producing a mere copy.
- **Fast Travel:** objective hidden canon assigns its removal to VRSAGIM's second-life design. Older attribution to Marzoth survives only as a possible religious/in-world interpretation.
- **Inventory:** there was never a dimensionless inventory grid before or after Lock-In.
- **Ghost HUD:** it deliberately survives as VRSAGIM's transitional mercy, but it does not restore names, age, titles, affiliations, nameplates, HP bars or oracle-like data.
- **Language:** unknown languages were never universally translated. Pre-Lock assistance improves with actual learning; Lock-In removes the artificial layer. Regional Common forms from the language atlas are restored to the text canon.
- **Dragon roster:** there are exactly twelve established true dragons. The stray Othmar Vey anecdote naming non-roster Vhrakzorrath is reconciled to canonical **Grimshokkuthrazkhan**. The explicitly discarded placeholder dragon names remain noncanon.
- **Reynold Buckley:** hidden canon establishes that InfernalGates4503 is the original Reynold preserved through VRSAGIM's first human continuity transfer, while Korigan's pre-Lock copy explanation remains the public/technical misinterpretation.
- **VRSAGIM motive:** stale “reserved for Phase VI” markers are superseded by VI.B's pity/second-chance/autonomy-failure motive.
- **Afterlife overstaying:** later canon resolves the broad mechanism as progressive failure of the living soul-body anchor, without a universal safe-duration timer.
- **Ghost-HUD intent:** stale “unresolved” markers are removed; its preservation is deliberate.
- **Divine-history mapping:** human writers did not coincidentally guess real metaphysics. VRSAGIM maps the human-authored fantasy canvas onto compatible pre-existing gods, divine remains and planes.
- **Demographic labels:** formerly “working final” demographic distributions are treated as canonical approximations rather than pending Phase-V figures.
- **Roadmap:** Phase VII is complete; Phase VIII is complete; Phase IX is next.

## 68.3 Intentional unknowns are not amnesia
The following are intentionally unresolved, optional, unknowable or simulation-dependent and must **not** be “repaired” by inventing definitive answers:
- the exact objective mechanics/criteria of post-mortal moral judgement;
- the final fate of a dead god's consciousness/soul;
- the complete distribution of every defeated god's powers;
- exact replacement-body/soul compatibility limits;
- repeated-resurrection upper limits and failure curves;
- the deep reason biological reinsertion is harder than extraction;
- whether Earth eventually develops reliable return technology;
- exact geopolitical paths by which the fictional 2057 Ukraine/Palestine premises arise;
- many cross-species reproductive combinations not already fixed;
- the ultimate origin of souls, the Three, and pre-divine reality;
- the full relationship between Dragonblooded players and individual true dragons beyond established possibility/consent;
- Vorthraktalyrionth's possible perception of presentation/system-layer phenomena;
- **Vhalemyr** as the provisional name of CaseOh's first bonded wyvern;
- **Cavill Watch** as an optional/provisional 2057 celebrity Easter egg rather than mandatory canon.

Unresolved possibility follows Phase IV.F: preserve it until causal simulation, explicit future canon or valid discovery resolves it. **Unknown is a state, not a prompt to improvise certainty.**

## 68.4 Canon versus character knowledge
Phase VIII reaffirms that the master canon and a character's beliefs are separate layers.

The simulator may know an objective fact while:
- the player character does not;
- an NPC believes the opposite;
- a government has classified it;
- the only evidence has been destroyed;
- scholars disagree over interpretation;
- or even VRSAGIM/the Three lack the deeper answer.

No audit correction becomes automatic in-world exposition.

## 68.5 Asset/reference hierarchy
Generated maps and language/script atlases remain valuable reference sources, but later textual corrections win over outdated labels. Exact generated pseudo-glyph shapes are not canonical encodings unless formally mapped. The absence of a fully encoded custom font/alphabet is an **asset-formalisation task**, not a world-canon contradiction and does not block the Master Instructions.

## 68.6 Phase VIII cardinal rule
> **Preserve established fact, explicitly supersede genuine contradictions, restore verified omitted canon, and protect intentional uncertainty from accidental invention. The Final Master Instructions must inherit one coherent world rather than every draft the project ever passed through.**

## 68.7 Phase VIII completion
**Phase VIII — Canon Reconciliation, Contradiction Audit & De-Amnesia Pass is complete.**

The project is ready to proceed to **Phase IX — Final Master Instructions**.

---

# 69. Phase IX — Sacred Master Instructions

Phase IX is complete. The final operating prompt has been created as **Aetheria_Online_Sacred_Master_Instructions.md**.

The Sacred Master Instructions are designed to be used alongside this Canon Tome. This Tome remains the detailed authority for established world facts; the Sacred Master Instructions govern how the simulator interprets GPTer actions, advances the world, resolves uncertainty, preserves continuity, presents information, and narrates scenes.

## 69.1 Final simulator architecture
The master prompt explicitly formalises:
- GPTer / player-character / in-world-player terminology;
- simulation-first causal resolution;
- canon hierarchy and contradiction handling;
- the three default campaign starts;
- biometric character creation and embodied species physiology;
- multi-resolution world simulation and a hidden continuity ledger;
- autonomous NPC and institutional decision-making;
- information provenance and character-knowledge limits;
- time, travel and downtime compression;
- anatomy-based combat and no encounter scaling;
- large-scale economic, political, military and ecological systems;
- pre-Lock game systems versus post-Lock lived reality;
- the deliberate Ghost HUD;
- language comprehension and naming rules;
- death, Afterlife, resurrection, Crossers and return-to-Earth limits;
- hidden-lore secrecy;
- narrative voice, dialogue, romance, tone and GPTer preferences;
- Tomas Bell's narrator-awareness exception;
- OOC/meta interaction;
- anti-patterns preventing generic MMO/fantasy behaviour;
- a response-construction loop and compact deployment core.

## 69.2 Master cardinal instruction
The final simulator ends on the principle:

> **Run the world. Do not write destiny.**

This phrase complements the existing cardinal rules:

> **Simulation first. Narrative second. Continuity always.**

> **Established fact — preserve it. Unresolved possibility — simulate it. Unspecified detail — invent cautiously.**

## 69.3 Project state
**Phases I–IX are complete.**

Future work is implementation, playtesting, optional asset formalisation, and later canon expansion rather than another required design phase.

Aetheria visual artefacts previously generated/available include:
- **Aetheria: The Enchanted World Map**
- **Aetheria: The Aetherial World Map**
- **Aetheria: Vaeloria’s Expanded Frontier**
- **Aetherian Common Language Atlas**
- **The Scripts of Aetheria / Conlang Atlas**

Important:
- map visuals contain some earlier draft labels/spellings;
- later textual correction wins;
- exact custom alphabet glyphs in generated images are visual references, **not deterministic canonical encodings** unless formally mapped later.

# 44. Important future formalisation tasks

## 44.1 Alphabets
The user wants the scripts eventually formalised as actual text-compatible encodings so future users immediately see the depth of work.

Best future route:
- canonical transliteration,
- deterministic glyph-to-sound tables,
- script reference codex,
- not relying on generated-image pseudo-text.

## 44.2 Persistent backup discipline
Because this project is extremely large, the living canon backup should be updated **after every completed subphase**, not merely after each major phase.

The goal is to prevent detail loss in long-context development and provide a durable source of truth.

## 44.3 Canon safety
Whenever older context and newer detail conflict:
- preserve both in notes if historically useful,
- mark the older version superseded,
- use newest explicit user correction as canon.
